# 28 — Implementation Status

> **What this file is:** the single map from the design corpus (docs 01–30; the design corpus proper is 01–25) to implementation reality. Every status here is backed by a command that was actually run. **Agents:** [`AGENT-DOC-NAVIGATION.md`](AGENT-DOC-NAVIGATION.md) → [`FULL-BACKLOG-2026-11-13.md`](FULL-BACKLOG-2026-11-13.md) (complete queue) → this file §3 (rulings through **363**). **Goal:** `GOAL-PROGRESS.md` — **NOT COMPLETE**. Header counts below = **historical 2026-11-10**. Steal evidence: `30-steal-list.md`.
>
> Status ladder per `docs/_conventions.md` §8: **`DESIGNED`** → **`CODE_PRESENT`** → **`WIRED`** → **`TESTED`** (automated tests exist and pass) → **`VERIFIED`** (the integrator ran the evidence command and observed the result; agent reports alone never reach `VERIFIED`) → **`RELEASED`**. Only `TESTED` and `VERIFIED` are claimed below; a level above what the evidence supports is never claimed, and an unknown is written `UNKNOWN`, never an implied success.
>
> Reconciled 2026-11-07 (reaudit stage 2, RC-15). Hardened 2026-11-07 (trust-hardening pass: chain anchors, refusal semantics, symlink-resolved scopes, canonical-JSON parity — §3 rulings 11–13, §4 gap 7; then V2 wave 1: NF-072 parallel tool batches — §1 row, ruling 14; then V2 wave 2: NF-073 loop safety rails — §1 row, ruling 15; then V2 wave 3: NF-069/052 edit-application ladder — §1 row, ruling 16; then the CI gate runner + ops tree (pass-5 C.3) — §1 row, ruling 17; then V2 waves 8–9: NF-071 managed runtime — §1 row, ruling 22 — and kernel R1 guard precedence + keyed anchors — ruling 23, ruling 12 amended; then V2 wave 10: the audit-response wave (protected kernel state, admissions for every tool, intent-bound approvals, shell exclusivity, durability pragmas, keyed Code Mode frames; plus the hermetic selftest) — §1 row, rulings 24–25; then V2 wave 11: canonical state paths (RC-17) — §1 row, ruling 26 — the NF-075 growth-loop v1 — ruling 27 — and evals-from-post-mortems — ruling 28; then V2 wave 12: the distrust-reaudit response (authority hardening, runtime/store hardening, audit-trail integrity, the kernel append tail cache) — §1 rows, rulings 29–31; then V2 wave 13: project memory (the AGENTS.md/ZH.md hierarchy), the eval runner, and one-use leases + session grants — §1 rows, ruling 32).

Live backlog: [`HANDOFF-BACKLOG-2026-11-13.md`](HANDOFF-BACKLOG-2026-11-13.md). Rulings §3 authoritative through **342** (body); header counts are archive.

> **Measured counts at this snapshot (2026-11-10, as root; every other count in this file that predates it is dated or labelled "wave-time" and is history, not current).** `cd app && bun run typecheck` 0 errors. `cd app && bun test`: **1800 tests in 148 files; 1792 pass / 8 skip / 0 fail** (`tests/core` 943 tests in 84 files with the 8 skips, `tests/tools` 455 in 30, `tests/providers` 102 in 9, `tests/tui` 300 in 25). The 8 skips are chmod based tests that cannot bind for uid 0 (rulings 39 and 51); the non-root run was not repeated in this pass (2026-11-09: zero failures as uid 1000). `cd kernel && cargo test --locked`: **49 unit + 50 integration pass**. `hz-kernel --selftest --state-dir <fresh dir>`: **`SELFTEST OK (25 checks)`**. `bash ops/gates.sh`: **eight gates, all passed as root** (rust-kernel-suite, kernel-selftest, ts-typecheck, ts-suite, e2e-headless-smoke, e2e-budget-rail, e2e-usage-error, startup-latency). Surfaces: 18 statically registered tools (`app/src/tools/index.ts`, plus dynamic `mcp__<server>__<tool>`), 26 reserved slash names (`BUILTIN_COMMANDS`; 23 rows in `/help`), 7 server methods (`SERVER_METHODS`), 11 kernel methods (`KERNEL_METHODS`, equal to the 11 match arms in `kernel/src/protocol.rs`), 91 numbered rulings in §3 (1 to 91, plus the extra ruling 9a). Pull requests: #1 to #29, listed in §5 (#29 is docs only).

| **Audit-response hardening - runtime, tools, stores** (wave 12) - minimiser preserves CRLF, strips bidi/zero-width controls, and spills the pre-minimisation raw bytes; `cat` interception is quote/glob-correct; the interactive blocklist skips `env`/`command`/`timeout`-style wrappers; Code Mode frames are key-anchored anywhere on a line with a bounded (64 KiB) buffer and truncation markers; `edit` refuses a stale preimage (docs/09 §8.2); preview cwd is workspace-confined, its approval names cwd + resolved command, and previews are process-global (close() stops all); the session DB quarantines corruption (`recovered`), every state store is 0600/0700, the spawn ledger reports torn tails without losing history, admission refuses cross-session id collisions, and the kernel appends through a warm tail cache (p50 0.53–0.6 ms flat at 2.2 k and 20 k records; was ~8 ms at 2.2 k) with truncation/tamper semantics now verified down to an in-place same-length/same-mtime-tick rewrite (see 2026-11-09 note) | **TESTED + VERIFIED** | `bun test tests/core/{durability-perms,codemode-preview,codemode-frames,managed-runtime}.test.ts tests/tools/` green; full suite (historical, wave-era) 607 / 0 / 48 files; `cargo test` 9 + 31; `ops/gates.sh` 6/6 | Ruling 30. **2026-11-09 (branch `fix/kernel-warm-tail-cache`):** the earlier "tamper semantics bit-exact" claim was false - a `(len, mtime)` cache hit returned the cached tail without re-reading disk, so an in-place rewrite of the last record that preserved byte length and landed within the same mtime tick (forging the `hash` to 64 chars) was masked and a subsequent append was wrongly allowed. Fixed in `kernel/src/eventlog.rs`: on a `(len, mtime)` hit `tail_of` now re-reads ONLY the cached last-record byte region and confirms its `seq`/`hash` still match before trusting the cache; on any mismatch/read/parse failure it drops the entry and falls through to a full rescan so the forged tail is seen and the anchor check refuses the append. Regression test `eventlog::tests::warm_cache_still_refuses_a_rewritten_tail` now passes; `cargo test` 9 unit + 35 integration green, `hz-kernel --selftest` SELFTEST OK (18 checks). Residuals: `setsid` descendants escape group kills (NF-016); `write` can be raced into creating an *empty* file in the state dir (no bytes); non-Linux fallbacks documented as weaker |
| **Audit-trail integrity** (wave 12) - an event-append failure notifies once, is counted, and forces the run's exit off `succeeded` (`internal_error`, message `event log append failed N time(s): <err>`); a receipt-issuance failure keeps its notice with the same guarantee; a transcript write failure after a tool effect marks the card failed, notices "the effect already ran", attempts the receipt best-effort, and raises `TranscriptAppendError` - never a bare "failed unexpectedly" | **TESTED + VERIFIED** | `bun test tests/core/logging-hardening.test.ts tests/tools/tools-hardening.test.ts` green; full suite (historical, wave-era) 607 / 0 / 48 files | Ruling 31 |

New guard kind `mcp.invoke` in BOTH kernels (Rust `KNOWN_KINDS` + `DEFAULT_POLICY` + `parse_rule`; TS mirror) with unknown-kind refusal parity intact and an interop pin driving both transports to identical allow/ask/deny decisions. Tool names `mcp__<serverId>__<toolName>`; parameters are the server's `inputSchema` verbatim; the description carries the untrusted fence (server text is data); `readOnlyHint` absent/false ⇒ mutating; results are fenced as data. v1 was user-config-only at wave 14; trusted workspace `mcp.json` may merge (rulings 105/113); malformed entries are skipped with a notice | **TESTED + VERIFIED** | `cargo test` → 9 + 35 green; interop pin green (release kernel rebuilt); `tests/core/mcp-client.test.ts` + fixture echo server green; full suite (historical, wave-era) 802 / 0 / 66 files (wave-time) | Ruling 34. Points now wired (each 1:1 to an H1 point): `tool.pre` (the only DECIDING point), `tool.post`, `session.start`/`session.end`, `step.start`/`step.end` (via `turn.start`/`turn.end`), `compact.pre`/`compact.post`, and the subagent child `session.start`/`session.end` boundary. A `tool.pre` hook may only narrow; a pre-hook failure (timeout, non-zero exit, garbage/missing/extra output, spawn error, stray `context`) DENIES with a typed reason naming the hook and carrying its STDERR TAIL to the model; a malformed config is FAIL-CLOSED (every call denied naming file + error); every other point is OBSERVATION-only (failures are notices, never block); first deny short-circuits; hooks run after Guard/approval, before the tool, via `spawnOwned`. | **Server mode (single-attach, NDJSON JSON-RPC v1)** (2026-11-09 server-rewind wave; docs/14 §3.2 reduced v1) - `hzcode serve` + `app/src/core/server.ts` bring up a long-lived leader for one workspace that speaks the ADDITIVE server protocol in `contracts.ts` (`HZ_PROTO=1`, `ServerRequest`/`ServerResponse`/`ServerNotification`, `SERVER_METHODS`; the kernel `KernelRequestMap` is UNCHANGED): one request line in, one response line out, plus `session.event` notifications carrying a `RuntimeEvent` tagged with its `sessionId`. The transport is INJECTED (`{input, output}` - a Node `Readable` or an async iterable of lines/bytes), so the suite drives it over in-memory pipes with no real stdio; `serve` wires it to `process.stdin`/`stdout` with stdout carrying ONLY protocol lines (diagnostics to stderr, S15) and SIGTERM → graceful `stop()` (every live runtime closed, exit 143). Methods: `server.hello` (reduced S35 handshake → `{proto, version, workspace, kernelMode, capabilities}`), `session.create` (fresh runtime), `session.list` (`hz://session/<id>` addressing, S8 - READ-ONLY: it opens a `SessionStore` for a read or reuses a live runtime and NEVER creates a session, so a bare `list` on an empty workspace returns `[]` and materializes nothing), `session.subscribe` (single-attach, last subscriber wins), `session.prompt` (drives `runtime.send`, streams every `RuntimeEvent`, replies `{text, aborted, exit}`), `session.rewind` (`runtime.rewindTo(n)` summary); an unknown method returns a JSON-RPC `method_not_found` error, never ignored (S89 spirit). Resume is BY ID: `runtimeFor(id)` opens `createRuntime({ sessionId: id })` so ANY session `session.list` advertised - not only the most-recently-touched one - can be prompted/subscribed; an unknown id fails closed as `INVALID_PARAMS`. An idless/malformed frame is answered with JSON `null` id (the unmatchable JSON-RPC id), never `0` (which is a legal correlation id). EVERY session is opened through `createRuntime` and nowhere else - so attach/resume runs BOTH crash recoveries (`repairDanglingToolCalls` + `recoverInterrupted`) before the next turn: this is the reduced `daemon_recovery` / `rollout_reconstruction` seam (codex app-server) + dsh `ToolCallRecovery`, re-deriving live state from the SessionStore + the APPEND-ONLY kernel event-log. All mutation goes through the runtime/kernel path - no server-only DB writes (S4/S49). This closes the server-resume reuse the crash-recovery row already anticipated (it named the server-mode resume path as reusing the same `createRuntime` entry point) | **TESTED + VERIFIED** | `cd app && bun test tests/core/server.test.ts` → 8 pass (hello/proto; create+prompt(mock) streaming `assistant.done`/`turn.done` + a reply with text/exit - the revert check; subscribe streams a turn; resume/attach a second server lists + continues the prior session; crash-reconstruct seeds a dangling tool_call + a `running` admission, attaches, and reads BOTH repaired back through a SECOND SQLite connection; `session.list` on a FRESH empty workspace returns `[]` and creates NO session - revert check reads the store back and fails if a session was materialized; resume-by-id prompts the OLDER of two sessions by its id - revert check fails if `runtimeFor` falls back to latest-only; unknown-method error); full suite (historical, wave-era) 860 pass / 13 known root/uid-0 baseline fails (ruling 39), zero new failures | Reduced v1, residuals NAMED (contracts.ts block + `server.ts` header): stdio only - no UDS/pipe, TCP loopback, HTTP/SSE/WS (S32); no auth (S38) or discovery/registration file (S34); no multi-client coordination - roles (S41), per-client cursors/lanes, driver-lease claim/release (S42–S44); the ACP bridge (S89) and the remaining §3.2 method families are DESIGNED extension points, not implemented. Single-attach (last subscriber per session wins) is the reduced stand-in for multi-client coordination; resume/crash-reconstruct reuse `createRuntime`'s recovery. **Review follow-up (same wave, closes the v1 non-blocking findings):** (1) resume is now BY ID (`RuntimeOptions.sessionId` added ADDITIVELY in `contracts.ts`; `createRuntime` opens that id or fails closed) so `session.list`'s per-id addressing is honest - no longer latest-only; (4) `session.list` is read-only and never creates a session; (3) idless/malformed frames answer with `null` id (`ServerResponse.id` relaxed to `number \| string \| null` - ADDITIVE, correlated responses unchanged) instead of `0`; (2) the inert `subscribed` set carries an explicit "not consulted in v1" note at the `notify` read site (single-attach streams unconditionally; the set is the S42–S44 per-connection fan-out seam) |
| **Turn-diff surface** (FEAT-003; research §1.11/§3, codex `turn_diff_tracker.rs` reference) - ADDITIVE `RuntimeEvent` variant `{ type: "turn.diff"; turn; files; diff; truncated? }` on the harness-side union in `app/src/contracts.ts`, justified in-comment as a computed UI/runtime FACADE event exactly like `context.usage`/`turn.done` - it is ephemeral (never written/projected, no seq/hash chain), so the CLOSED 44-name kernel catalogue (`docs/_conventions.md` §6) is UNCHANGED and no kernel event is added. Aggregator `app/src/core/turn-diff.ts` (`computeTurnDiff(entries, maxLines?)`) collapses the change-journal entries recorded DURING one model turn into ONE unified diff: one net diff per file, `before` from the EARLIEST entry for that file (null = new file), `after` read once from disk at turn end (several edits to the same file ⇒ one net diff), REUSING `unifiedDiff` from `core/plan.ts` (byte-stable, 3-line context) - no new diff algorithm. Non-restorable/`beforeUnreadable` preimages are noted inline (diff-from-empty), a disk read that fails (deleted file) is a safe empty `after`, output is byte-stable (files in first-change order) under a total line cap (`truncated`). `change-journal.ts` gains ONE ADDITIVE export `liveEntries(sessionId)` (a fresh copy of the already-merged live view `lastChange` reads - no existing signature/behaviour changed). `loop.ts` (minimal, additive): captures the session's journal entry-id boundary just BEFORE the turn's `batchSegments` tool batch, then after the batch computes the diff for the entries appended since the boundary and emits exactly one `turn.diff` via `emit(deps.callbacks, …)` - ONLY when `files > 0` (a read-only turn emits none; plan-mode staged writes do NOT record to the journal, so they correctly emit none); best-effort (a failure never breaks the run). `tui/render.ts` needs no change (its `default` arm already no-ops unknown RuntimeEvents, like `context.usage`) | **TESTED** | `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors; `cd app && bun test tests/core/turn-diff.test.ts tests/core/turn-diff-loop.test.ts` → 14 pass (two files in one turn ⇒ one diff, files===2; two edits to one file collapse to earliest-before→final-after; zero changes ⇒ files===0/empty; valid unified diff `--- /+++ /@@`; new-file `/dev/null`; nonRestorable noted; deleted-file safe; byte-stable order; cap⇒truncated; journal-boundary path; loop emits exactly one `turn.diff` files===1 for a one-write turn, none for a text-only turn, none for a plan-mode staged write); full suite (historical, wave-era) 865 pass (branch-time; integrated: 1250 pass / 11 known root-sandbox fail) / 13 fail (the 13 are the pre-existing root/uid-0 permission artifacts, not regressions - ruling 39) | Ruling 46. Update (ruling 54): the TUI now renders the turn diff; the per-file cap (200 lines) and total cap (400 lines) are fixed constants |

| **models.dev catalog seam** (provider-superset FEAT-003; spec `research/compare-providers.md` §5.4) - a model catalog abstraction replaces the *usage* of the 5-string `DEFAULT_MODELS` with a data-driven lookup. The record mirrors opencode's normalized shape REDUCED to the fields HorizonCode reads - per model `{ id, provider, limit:{context, output?}, capabilities?:{tools?, reasoning?}, cost? }`, per provider a `defaultModel` id. Decoding is TOLERANT (mirrors opencode `models-dev.ts normalize()`): an unparseable model is SKIPPED and collected in `rejected`, never fatal; a top-level non-object yields an empty catalog with one rejection line. `loadCatalog(env)` is a three-way SEAM with precedence (1) `HZ_MODELS_SNAPSHOT` env file override (external → floor-validated) → (2) the committed curated snapshot (boot-time offline floor, NOT floor-validated) → (3) a documented-but-NOT-default live-fetch extension point `fetchLiveCatalog` (would GET `CATALOG_PATH = /experimental/v2.0/api.json?type=chat` from `https://models.opencode.ai`/models.dev; DISABLED under no-network, NEVER called from `loadCatalog` or tests - it throws when called directly). `validateCatalog(catalog, {minProviders})` enforces the `MINIMUM_PROVIDERS = 100` floor (opencode's "a floor, not equality" guard) ONLY on externally-sourced snapshots (override/fetch); the small curated set is deliberately exempt (documented in the module header + here). `config.ts` default-model resolution for built-ins now goes through `builtinDefaultModel(provider, catalog)` = `catalogDefaultModel(...) ?? DEFAULT_MODELS[provider]`; `DEFAULT_MODELS` is KEPT with byte-identical values (claude-sonnet-4-5 / gpt-4o-mini / gemini-2.5-flash / llama3.1 / mock-1) as the offline fallback, and the curated snapshot carries the same ids so the resolved default equals the constant. DOCTRINE: capabilities/limits are DATA from the record - no `id.includes()`/model-name regex anywhere in the module. DELIBERATE DEFERRAL: the full ~8 MB models.dev snapshot bundle (opencode ships 230 providers / 8787 models) and the live refresh (ETag/digest, 5-minute poll) are OUT OF SCOPE for this PR - too heavy for review and unavailable under the no-network sandbox; | **Interactive PTY exec** (`shell_session`; codex `unified_exec` exec_command + write_stdin, convergent with oh-my-pi bash-interactive/bash-pty-selection) - an ADDITIVE, fail-closed interactive terminal the model can drive: `mode:start` runs a command in a bounded (`MAX_SESSIONS=4`) persistent session returning a session id + buffered output, `mode:write` feeds stdin to a running session, `mode:close` kills it. Every start is admitted through the kernel guard as a `shell.exec` effect (loop `guardActionFor` maps `start`→`shell.exec`, `write`/`close`→session-state exactly like preview; the tool ALSO calls `ctx.guard`/`ctx.approve` - "Run interactive command" - defence in depth) - a `deny` refuses WITHOUT spawning, an `ask` routes through approval. `concurrencyClass: "exclusive"` (R-tools-39, proven in batch.test.ts; no loop.ts concurrency change). PTY-vs-piped is capability-detected: when util-linux `script` is present and `HZ_NO_PTY` is unset the command runs under `script -qec <cmd> /dev/null` (real /dev/pts tty), otherwise the piped `bash -lc` fallback (the always-works headless path). `canUseInteractivePty` is pure/injectable. Spawned through `spawnOwned` (longLived:false, detached:true, own process group, `stdin:"pipe"` so write works, `scrubEnv()`), killed with shell.ts's SIGTERM-grace-then-SIGKILL ladder to the GROUP; bounded idle + overall/wall timeouts (DEFAULT 120_000 / MAX 600_000). Output MATCHES codex: `Exit code:`/`Signal:`, `Wall time: <s>s` (0.1s rounding), `Total output lines:` ONLY when the 30_000-char buffer capped, then `Output:` + minimised text (shell.ts minimiser imported, shell.ts untouched); over-budget buffers spill via `planToolOutputCap`/`writeSpillFile`, with the raw spill capture SCOPED PER READ WINDOW (`rawForSpill` is reset in `takeBuffer` in lockstep with `buffer`, so it neither grows unbounded over a long-lived session nor spills stale whole-session history). The one-shot `shell.ts` and its `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKLIST` are left completely intact; this is the explicit opt-in for interactivity. | **TESTED + VERIFIED** | `cd app && bun test tests/tools/interactive-exec.test.ts` → 17 pass (piped round-trip start→write_stdin→close, codex output format incl. truncated/non-truncated, per-read raw-spill scoping, session cap refusal, idle/overall timeout kill, clean close kill, guard deny/ask admission, the pure PTY-capability helper, loop admission + concurrency, and a PTY integration case asserting the child sees `/dev/pts/`); `bun test tests/core/batch.test.ts` → 7 pass (`concurrencyClassOf("shell_session") === "exclusive"`); `bun test tests/core/authority-response.test.ts` → 10 pass / 3 skip (registry fully mapped); full suite (historical, wave-era) **860 pass / 36 skip / 13 fail / 76 files** (the 13 are the documented root/uid-0 permission-enforcement + kernel-parity artifacts of ruling 39 - unchanged from the FEAT-001 baseline, +17 pass from the new tests); reverting `interactive-exec.ts` makes the new behavioural tests fail. | Additive surface: none - reuses the existing `shell.exec` GuardAction (contracts.ts unchanged). Loop change is minimal + additive (the `shell_session` admission mapping, mirroring preview; required so the loop's fail-closed admission does not refuse the new tool). cd kernel && cargo test --locked && ./target/release/hz-kernel --selftest

# TypeScript: typecheck 0 errors; suite 1800 tests / 148 files (2026-11-10)

printf 'list files' | ./bin/hzcode

| `app/tests/core` | 84 files, 943 tests | trusted client vs Rust kernel: chained appends with independently recomputed hashes, read windows, tamper, receipts, policy/lease flow; TS fallback + both-direction interop (incl. canonical-JSON astral-key parity, lease-pattern parity, refusal semantics, chain anchors); symlink-resolved workspace scopes; config precedence; sessions; skills + subagents; code mode + preview; tool batches (NF-072: classes, barriers, parallel timing, keyed approvals); loop rails (NF-073: budgets checked pre-dispatch, charged failures, wall clock, format cap, countdown, ledger sharing; NF-071: spawn lint, worker identity, owner-death supervisor, admission FIFO; the audit-response suite and the Code Mode frame-key suite; the wave-11 suites: state paths, growth loop, post-mortems/evals; wave-12: kernel hardening, logging hardening, durability/permissions; the provider registry: tolerant `parseProviderRegistry`, `loadConfig` resolution of a registered id to the openai-compatible adapter end to end) |

## 3. Semantics rulings made during implementation (binding)

1. **Issued leases are honoured by both implementations.** The kernel (`guard.rs`) consults caller-supplied leases (expiry-filtered) ∪ internally held leases; the TS fallback (`LocalKernel.checkGuard`) merges the same way. An expired *copy* passed by a caller cannot revoke a held lease — expiry happens by time. Tests assert this ordering deliberately.
2. **`arguments` is always a JSON string.** No-input tool calls produce `{}`, never `""` (both adapters fixed).
3. **`log.read` `limit` is strict** — `limit:0` returns zero events (kernel fix).
4. **Guard defaults** (no `policy.json`): `fs.read`/`fs.write` inside workspace → `allow`; outside → `ask`; `shell.exec`/`net.fetch`/`spawn` → `ask`. Precedence is not restated here: it is owned by `docs/11-authority-safety.md` R1 (the shipped guard's divergence is §4 gap 5).
5. **Headless approvals fail closed** (auto-deny with a notice). PTY/TUI prompts y/n/a (`a` = session lease).
6. **Zero-config default is the mock provider** — chat must work with no keys. Real providers via `HZ_PROVIDER` + keys or `~/.hzcode/config.json`.
7. **Delegation is an optional capability on the context.** `ToolContext.delegate?: DelegateRunner` — present on parent runs, absent in child runs; a subagent's tools are exactly the seven base tools (children are filtered in both `toolSpecs` and `getTool`).
8. **Skills activate per turn.** `/name rest` injects `## Active skill: name` + body (cap 8000) into the system prompt for that turn only and sends `rest` (falling back to `Apply the <name> skill.` when bare) as the user content; the directory section caps at 12 skills.
9. **The base system prompt is data.** `app/src/core/prompt/` holds the static sections (identity, loop, tools, safety, git, communication, quality; about 700 estimated tokens with the environment block, the test cap) and a request-time environment block (cwd, date, OS, shell, git yes/no, kernel trusted/untrusted, plan mode, the tools actually visible). Wire order: static prefix, environment, project memory, per-model block, rails. Subagents get a child prompt (task, tool subset, no questions, concise report, no inherited approvals) and now inherit the project instruction files. Snapshot goldens in `app/tests/core/fixtures/`.
9a. **Instruction files are peers with one precedence (stage 2).** `app/src/core/instructions.ts` loads, least to most specific (later wins; the user's chat wins over all): user `~/.config/horizon/` AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md, then per directory from repo root to cwd AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, CONVENTIONS.md; at the repo root also `.github/copilot-instructions.md`, `.cursorrules`, `.cursor/rules/*.mdc` and `.windsurfrules`; then `.hz/AGENTS.md`, `ZH.md`, and local overrides (`CLAUDE.local.md`, root to cwd). Order is position-major, so a nested AGENTS.md never precedes a root CLAUDE.md. Justification from the reference agents: AGENTS.md (codex, opencode, grok-build, oh-my-pi), CLAUDE.md and CLAUDE.local.md (claude-code, grok-build), GEMINI.md and `.github/copilot-instructions.md` (oh-my-pi), `.cursorrules`/`.cursor/rules` (oh-my-pi, grok-build, cline), `.windsurfrules` (oh-my-pi, cline). CONVENTIONS.md (aider's convention) has no listed reference; it is supported as a plain peer file. `.mdc` rules load only with `alwaysApply: true`; glob-scoped or description-only rules are listed under `/memory` as "not applied", because injecting a path-scoped rule globally would misstate its scope. Dedup: the same real path, or the same text under a different kind, loads once. The 32 KB/file and 128 KB total caps, symlink-escape refusal and "conventions, not authority" framing apply to every kind; each block is attributed (`--- rel (kind)`) under a short follow-instruction. **Lazy nested injection:** `LazyInstructions` registers the not-yet-loaded AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md in the ancestor chain (inside the repo) of any path a successful read/write/edit/apply_patch/ast_edit touches; each is loaded once (32 KB each, 64 KB per session; the lazy registry is in-memory, so it lasts for the life of the process and a restart or resume drops it until a tool touches the directory again), rendered as an attributed untrusted `<nested_instructions>` block on the system rail ahead of the other rails (append-only, stable prefix untouched), never a user message. **`/memory`** (alias `/instructions`) lists loaded files with kind, size, truncation, skips and unapplied rules. **`/init`** runs an ordinary turn that inspects the repo read-only and writes AGENTS.md through the normal `write` tool with plan mode on (staged; `/changes` to review, `/apply` writes through the normal guard, which asks only where it would ask for any write; the staged diff review is the user's approval of the content); without plan mode the write takes the normal guarded path. Tests: `tests/core/instruction-peers.test.ts`, `tests/core/instruction-hierarchy.test.ts`, `tests/tui/memory-init.test.ts`. Lazy fragments are held per session (a later user turn keeps them), a file skipped because the 64 KB lazy budget is used up raises a notice, and `/memory` lists the lazy fragments and budget skips for the current session. Discovery runs after a SUCCESSFUL path-touching call, so the call that first touches a directory (including a `write` or `apply_patch` Add File into an unread directory) runs without that directory's rules: the rules reach the next request, and a pre-call load cannot help a call the model has already issued. `grep`, `glob` and list do not trigger discovery. Identical text under another kind keeps the first (least specific) copy, which changes attribution only. The main-agent prompt order is static prefix, project memory, per-model block, environment, rails.
9. **Code mode is one bridge, no registry.** Programs see only `tools.call(name, args)`; the parent resolves against the base seven, guards and receipts every executed call; "unknown tool" is an ordinary call result.
10. **Previews are session-scoped process groups.** Spawned detached; stop SIGTERMs then SIGKILLs `-pid`; failures never leave orphans. The registry is in-memory per session.
11. **Kernel refusals are final.** `KernelClient` falls back to the local kernel only on transport failures (exit, timeout, malformed output, unknown request id). An error *response* from the kernel is surfaced to the caller and the mode stays `trusted`; a refusing guard fails closed at the `GuardGate`. Refusing was previously indistinguishable from unreachable.
12. **Chain anchors.** Every `*.jsonl` chain carries a `<file>.head` anchor (seq + hash of the last record), written atomically after each append. `log.verify`/`receipt.verify` compare the chain against it, and appends refuse when tail and anchor disagree. An absent anchor is a legacy chain and is accepted; §4 gap 7 records the residual. **Amendment (2026-11-07, wave 9):** anchors are optionally keyed — with `HZ_ANCHOR_KEY` (hex, ≥32 chars) each `<file>.head` stores `HMAC-SHA256(key, canonical record preimage)` instead of `sha256`, byte-for-byte in both implementations (RFC 2104 over the existing `sha2` crate, pinned by RFC 4231 vectors); a legacy (unkeyed or absent) anchor under a configured key fails closed with `HZ_PRECONDITION_FAILED: legacy (unkeyed) anchor …` unless `HZ_ACCEPT_LEGACY_ANCHORS=1` (verify accepts; the next append re-anchors keyed); a wrong key reports tamper (`brokenAt`), never ok; no key configured = byte-identical legacy behaviour. §4 gap 7 records the closed/residual split.
13. **Scopes and lease patterns.** `scopeFor` resolves symlinks (workspace and target, through the deepest existing ancestor) before classifying inside/outside. Lease patterns are exact or trailing-`*` only — identical in both implementations — and an empty pattern is refused rather than widened to `*`.
14. **Batches run by concurrency class (NF-072).** A batch is split into segments: consecutive `shared` calls run concurrently (`Promise.all`, all-settled — one call's failure cancels no sibling); an `exclusive` call is its own barrier. Every call still resolves, guards, leases and receipts individually, and the whole sequence lands on the same event chain. `GuardGate` serializes `check`/`approve`, and an approval passes the action it answers so concurrent asks cannot cross-wire. `concurrencyClass` is additive on `Tool`; absent means shared.
15. **Loop safety rails (NF-073).** Budgets (`RunBudget`: steps, tokens, wall clock) are checked in one sentinel before every provider dispatch, under one convention — `0` (or absent) is unlimited. A turn is charged before it runs, so a billed-but-failed turn still spends its step, and reported usage is charged even when the turn ends in an error. A run reports one RC-7 terminal outcome (`RunExit`); budget stops are `budget_exhausted`/`timeout` (exit 5 per the canonical table) and the wall clock is never reset or lifted. A batch in which no tool call carried parseable JSON is a format-error step; any clean call resets the streak; 3 consecutive steps fail the run (`format_errors`, exit 1). The corrective text rides the next step's system rail — the Anthropic adapter drops mid-history `system` roles, so it must not be a history message. `BudgetLedger` is one ceiling per run tree: subagents get a child slice that checks the parent first and charges both, serialized on a mutex. `RunBudget`/`RunExit` and the optional `budget` on `RuntimeOptions` are additive contract changes; `runtime.send` returns `exit?`.
16. **Edit-application ladder (NF-069/052 extensions, A-1…A-5).** When exact matching finds nothing and the reflection slot is unspent, `edit` escalates: relative-indent normalisation (strip each block's common prefix, compare, and re-indent the replacement by the window's prefix preserving relative depth) → 3-way (trim) merge (every line equal after `trim()`) → line-patch (first/last non-blank lines as anchors, same span length). A rung applies only when exactly one candidate exists; several candidates fail with the named rung and candidate line spans and no write. Failures return diff-shaped feedback — the closest actual window as `-`/`+` pairs with a similarity percentage, plus a REPLACE-already-present note with line numbers. The reflection slot (`MAX_EDIT_REFLECTIONS = 1`, keyed per session and file, cleared by a successful edit) allows one repair with that feedback; a second failure disables fuzzy matching for the file (exact matching still works). Error and success strings are a contract (A-5), pinned byte-identical in `tests/tools/edit-ladder.test.ts`; fuzzy successes append `(matched via <rung>)`, exact successes do not.
17. **CI gate runner and ops tree (pass-5 C.3).** `ops/gates.sh` runs the six gates that exist in this repository today (Rust suite, kernel selftest, TS suite, headless smoke asserting tool-exchange output and a non-empty receipts chain, budget-rail exit 5, usage-error exit 2) — fail-fast, temp-logged, hermetic (mock provider, throwaway HOME, no network). `ops/gate-catalogue.md` maps every one of the 18 `docs/24` §3.5 gates, all `declared-gap` with the missing surface named per row; a row flips to `runnable` only in the change that makes it run. The GitHub Actions workflow cannot be committed by the automation identity (its token has no `workflow` scope — the first push attempt was rejected on exactly that, and the unpublished commits were rewritten locally to drop the path; nothing was published, no force-push involved): it ships as the tracked payload `ops/ci/github-actions.yml`, installed by `ops/install-ci.sh`. It claims no §3.5 gate is enforced.
18. **Context-cost contracts (NF-074).** Every provider request's output cap is fitted from the last reported usage (`inputTokens + outputTokens` as the anchor base, plus locally estimated appended deltas): `min(8192 ceiling, window − anchor − 4096 headway)`, floored at 256; before any anchor exists the whole prompt is estimated locally (bytes/4 + 16-token framing per message, tool-call arguments and the tool-schema blob counted). Tool results are capped at 16 KiB / 4,000 tokens: truncation is line-aware and lossless-resumable — the kept region is a true prefix and `nextOffset` is its byte length — and the full text spills to `<ws>/.hzcode/spills/<sha256>.txt` with a marker pointer; a caller-supplied stream cap that is exceeded fails the call without spilling (never spill a clipped stream); receipts always digest the full output. The shell minimiser (ANSI/OSC, CR progress, trailing whitespace, blank runs) runs by default with a visible `[output minimised: …]` flag, byte-identical when nothing changed. `cat <file>` (exactly one plain argument, `VAR=` prefixes skipped) intercepts to the read tool with a `guard` `fs.read` admission and a visible notice; terminal programs and forever-stream tails (`tail -f`) are refused before Guard, with `HZ_ALLOW_INTERACTIVE=1` the documented override. Local estimates are for fitting only — never for accounting.
19. **Repo-map ranking (B-1, doc-09 §4.2 `repo_map`).** The map is a def/ref graph scored by personalised PageRank: definitions come from per-language lexical patterns (exported/`pub` declarations first, then locals — no tree-sitter dependency, the reduced form the doc's "no embeddings, no index service" clause allows), references are whole-identifier occurrence counts, and edge weight is sqrt(refs) × (1 + 50 for idents defined in focus files) × (10 for focus-file out-edges) — the aider weight table as the starting prior. Rendering binary-searches the ranked file list for the largest prefix within the token budget (default 4,000, hard 16,000) and appends `[repo_map: n files, ~t tokens]`, plus an explicit `[truncated: …]` marker when files are omitted. Files with no definitions never enter the body; test-shaped paths are excluded unless `includeTests`; output is byte-stable for a fixed tree+params (cacheable and comparable); one `fs.read` admission per call. Not built here: the persistent `hz_index` side (docs/10 R97/R103) — this tool is index-free by design.
20. **Canonical JSON numbers (docs/05 D40; closes pass-5 C.1).** The canonical number form is fixed as the IEEE-754 doubles serialized by the ECMAScript shortest-round-trip algorithm that `JSON.stringify` implements (`0.1` → `0.1`, `1e21` → `1e+21`), with negative zero normalised to `0`, `NaN`/`±Infinity` refused (never silently `null`), and integers at or beyond ±2^53 refused in decimal form. Enforced TypeScript-side by `validateCanonicalNumbers` in `app/src/core/canon.ts` — a walk invoked by `canonicalJson` that throws `CanonicalNumberError` naming the value's JSONPath-style location (`$.payload.limits.count`) before any serialization; all valid inputs stay byte-identical, so `hashEnvelope`/`hashReceipt`/`digestOf` are unchanged. Tests: `app/tests/core/canon-numbers.test.ts` (10). Residual, recorded: `contracts.ts`'s header and `kernel/src/canon.rs` still describe floats as outside the v0.1 interop contract, and the Rust serializer is not yet aligned — the next kernel wave carries that.
21. **Internal capability IDs (closes pass-5 C.2).** The doc-11 §4.1 runtime-internal IDs now sit in a labelled INTERNAL (non-canonical) block in `_conventions.md` §5: they MUST NOT cross any surface, MUST NOT satisfy a registry-parity check for a canonical ID, are promoted into the canonical list in the change that first implements their feature (RC-11), and are a defect if they outlive two revisions without promotion or withdrawal. `docs/24` §3.3's proposals keep their Reserved status and point at the block; nothing was promoted.
22. **Managed runtime v1 (NF-071).** Every owned child spawns through one `spawnOwned` (`core/owned-spawn.ts`): the ledger row (`<stateDir>/spawns.jsonl`) is appended before return, and a ledger that fails to parse is renamed `spawns.jsonl.corrupt-<epochMillis>` and read as `corrupt: true` — a refusal state, never an empty history (the spawn lint allows exactly one `Bun.spawn` site, and every allowlist entry must carry a reason). Identity is `{pid, startTicks}` from /proc field 22 (`core/worker-identity.ts`): a dead pid or reused pid is refused; ancestor-chain proving is deferred and stated in the module. A long-lived child must be detached and gets the poll-based `sh` owner-death supervisor (`core/supervisor.ts`, ~100 ms, `kill -9 -<worker-pgid>` on any owner death incl. SIGKILL/OOM). The admission FIFO (`core/admission.ts`; table created migration-safely in `session.ts`) admits once by id; an `unknown` row halts followers — `nextRunnable` refuses and only `resolve(requeue, drop or cancel)` unblocks — and `recoverInterrupted` settles `running`→`unknown` with evidence `interrupted`. Crash-recovery wiring (2026-11-09 server-rewind wave, external audit finding 2 closed): `createRuntime` now calls BOTH recoveries on session open/resume, before the first turn — `repairDanglingToolCalls` (TRANSCRIPT: the dsh ToolCallRecovery pairing invariant, wired since wave 10) and `recoverInterrupted` (ADMISSION FIFO: previously dead code on the live path, now invoked so a `running` row left by a killed turn becomes `unknown`/`interrupted` and halts followers instead of silently replaying). The two are complementary, not duplicative — one keeps the provider-replayable history valid, the other keeps the work-queue honest — and both are idempotent, so a repeated open is a no-op. Tests: `tests/core/crash-recovery.test.ts` (4, incl. a revert check that fails if the `recoverInterrupted` wiring is removed). Not in this slice: the framed owner/worker control pipe; the FIFO is consulted at open, not yet driving `loop.ts` dispatch turn-by-turn.
23. **Guard precedence is docs/11 R1 (closes §4 gap 5).** deny > lease > ask > allow: a matching ask rule beats a matching allow rule in either declaration order, in `kernel/src/guard.rs` and the TS fallback; lease semantics unchanged (an unexpired matching lease still allows, and still beats an ask). Tests `guard_ask_rule_beats_allow_rule` and `guard_lease_still_beats_a_matching_ask_rule`; no prior expectation encoded the old order. Residual, recorded: `app/src/contracts.ts`'s `DEFAULT_POLICY` comment still stated the old order — one-line follow-up (fixed in this same wave; see §4 gap 5).
24. **Audit-response wave (external review triage, 2026-11-07).** The kernel state directory is structurally protected: `guard.rs` denies any `fs.write` resolving inside it (deepest existing ancestor canonicalised; `..` and symlinks covered) before policy and leases, reason `kernel-state-protected`; both TS transports refuse earlier with `HZ_PRECONDITION_FAILED: kernel-state-protected: …`; `policy.json` hot reload stays for human edits — residual: a raw `shell` redirect is not path-classified (§4 gap 10). Every tool now passes an admission: `agent` → `spawn` (task head ≤120 chars), `code_mode` → `spawn` (`code_mode <sha256 head>`), and a tool without a mapping is refused with `HZ_PRECONDITION_FAILED: no admission mapping for tool …` before execution. Subagents receive no parent session leases (docs/09 R-tools-32) and re-enter approval; the GuardGate approval cache keys full intent (`kind + target + sha256(canonical args)`) so a different write to the same file re-asks. `shell` carries `concurrencyClass: "exclusive"` (R-tools-39). The session store opens `journal_mode=WAL` + `synchronous=FULL` beside `busy_timeout` (RC-16; migration-safe). Tests: `app/tests/core/authority-response.test.ts` (12) plus updated batch/session/skills suites; `kernel/tests/integration.rs::guard_denies_writes_inside_the_state_dir_even_when_policy_allows`.
25. **Code Mode control frames are keyed (closes triage finding 5's framing half).** Every shim frame is one line `<key> <json>`, 64-hex key; the parent treats only key-prefixed lines as control frames and everything else — whoever wrote it — as program output, so `process.stdout.write('{"done":true}')` cannot end a run early. Two keys: a bootstrap nonce in `HZ_CODEMODE_FRAME_KEY` (read and deleted before model code exists, retired after exactly one handshake line — the environment block stays readable on Linux, which is why it frames one line and nothing more), then an in-process run key that never touches env/argv/files; the parent withholds the program until the handshake is accepted, and frames go through the stdout writer captured at load. Residuals, named in the child header and measured, not assumed: ambient process/Bun access, direct fd writes (output, never frames), the bootstrap nonce readable after the fact but retired, in-process reflection, and everything outside the bridge — Code Mode remains bridge-policed, not OS-sandboxed (NF-016 is the confinement work). Tests: `app/tests/core/codemode-frames.test.ts` (forged `done` is log output; env key scrubbed; per-run key differs; stdout read-back refused — socketpair stdio, verified).
26. **Canonical state paths, canonical-first with in-place legacy (closes §4 gap 6; RC-17).** One resolver, `app/src/core/paths.ts`: an existing `<workspace>/.hzcode/` is honoured in place forever — shipped relative names kept, nothing under `.hz` created, legacy wins when both exist — else the canonical tree: kernel `<ws>/.hz/state/kernel` (RC-16(f)), session DB `<ws>/.hz/state/hzcode.db` (docs/12 §4.1), and `spills/`, `skills/`, `history` mirrored under `.hz/state/` (judgement call, recorded); user config: existing `~/.hzcode/` in place, else `~/.config/horizon/`. All call sites routed with exported names/signatures unchanged. Tests: `state-paths.test.ts` (8) incl. symlinked workspace and HOME; fresh-workspace smoke through the trusted Rust kernel. Residuals: legacy→canonical migration is explicit future work; the standalone kernel's default state dir (no `--state-dir`) still names `~/.hzcode/kernel`; per-call resolution means a mixed-version concurrent run can still fork (stated in the resolver header).
27. **Growth loop v1 (NF-075, reduced form).** `core/review.ts` builds a review-run SPEC, never a loop: the fork's system prefix IS the parent's recorded prefix bytes and a stale/mismatched sha256 refuses with `prefix-parity-mismatch`; the tool subset is closed and read-only (`read`/`grep`/`glob`, `fs.read`); the review instruction rides the tail (ADR-009/NF-005); every record carries `origin: review-fork`. `core/curator.ts` is the editorial standard as one function — one typed refusal per proposal (shape/kind → title → body → authority-widening → do-not-capture → evidence → read-before-write) — and `curate()` appends canonical-JSON rows to `<stateDir>/proposals.jsonl`, dedupes by sha256({kind,title,body}), refuses an unreadable store (`store-corrupt`, never read as empty), and applies nothing (ADR-010). Title ≤ 120 / body ≤ 4,000 are the v1 numbers (NF-075 gives none; docs/30 G-3's 60-char render budget awaits the render path). Not in this slice: post-turn scheduling, loop wiring, provider calls, proposal application, and the curator's archive/snapshot/pin half.
28. **Evals from post-mortems (docs/30 §4 item 8, reduced form).** One `<stateDir>/postmortems/<epochMillis>-<shortid>.json` per failed run: RC-7 terminal, `RunExit.detail`, a closed failure class, the transcript RANGE (first/last seq + count) with sha256 of its text and `digestOf` of the failure signature, name→count tools, first user-visible task line (300-char cap). An unparseable file quarantines to `<file>.corrupt-<epochMillis>` (`corrupt: true`), never read as empty. `core/evals.ts` builds local `PostmortemEvalCase`s (NOT docs/05 `EvalCaseV1`) with an enforced answer-isolation precondition (refusals: `no_task`, `bad_source`, `answer_leak`, `bad_forbidden`) and publishes counts through `metricContract()` (H-7). Remaining from item 8: the eval runner (cases are data), live loop wiring, test-model-in-library.
29. **Audit-response hardening (distrust reaudit, 2026-11-08; wave 12).** Authority: the state-dir rule resolves every path component through symlinks lexically (no existence requirement, hop-limited, fails closed) in `guard.rs` and `kernel.ts`; `write`/`edit` perform the effect through an opened descriptor re-classified against the state dir before any byte on Linux (`/proc/self/fd`, verify-then-allow — a symlink through to a legitimate target keeps working; refusal names both paths); `policy.json` is refused while `nlink > 1` (hardlinks are invisible to path checks) and a malformed rule refuses service naming its index; leases carry an owner (`sessionId` on mint + check; owner-less legacy leases remain globally consultable by design) so subagent children and new sessions re-enter approval; caller-supplied lease objects are claims, never authority; unknown action kinds are refused before evaluation; approvals key the full intent and a parent admission answers only the same kind AND target; `always` grants name the exact approved target/command with the real 15-minute session window. Tests: `app/tests/core/kernel-hardening.test.ts` (new), interop/fallback/authority-response/tui-approval extensions, Rust integration. Residuals: legacy owner-less leases; child re-approval over the Rust stdio transport proven at kernel layer; non-policy state files remain hardlink-writable (chain detection covers); the class fix is kernel-executed effects (NF-016).
30. **Runtime, tools and store hardening (same wave).** Stated as the §1 rows; the residuals worth naming: `setsid` descendants still escape group kills (best-effort `/proc` sweeps cover the ledger-failure path), `write` can be raced into creating an empty file inside the state dir (no bytes written), and non-Linux targets take documented weaker fallbacks — all owned by NF-016.
31. **Audit-trail failures fail the run (same wave).** An event-log append failure notifies once, is counted, and forces the exit off `succeeded` (`internal_error`, message `event log append failed N time(s): <err>`); a receipt-issuance failure keeps its notice with the same guarantee; a transcript-append failure after a tool effect marks the card failed, records the effect, attempts the receipt, and raises `TranscriptAppendError` so the run stops typed — never a bare "failed unexpectedly". Tests `logging-hardening.test.ts` (3) + the `tools-hardening.test.ts` transcript case.
32. **One-use leases + session grants (wave 13; closes the last half of triage finding 4).** docs/11 L1/L7 reduced form. A held lease is consumed by the first matching allowance — taken out of the table, cited with a `(consumed)` reason; a repeat falls back to policy or a grant, never to the spent lease. The "always" answer mints a session **grant** (`guard.grant` — a new protocol op in both kernels; owner-scoped and expiring like a lease) that allows nothing by itself: where a check would ask, it mints a fresh one-use lease BOUND to the exact action (pattern = the resolved target, TTL `min(120 s, grant expiry)`), which the decision returns and the receipt cites — and which is deliberately NOT added to the consultable table, because holding it let one approval admit two effects (caught by the kernel test against the first draft). Precedence unchanged: deny > held lease > grant > ask > allow; a grant never beats a deny rule and never fires where an allow rule answered. TUI "always" (`src/tui/approval.ts`) and runtime `grantLease()` (`src/core/index.ts`) mint grants. Tests: `kernel/tests/integration.rs` (consumption, mint-per-effect, grant-vs-deny/allow — 9 + 34 green), `app/tests/core/{interop,kernel-hardening,fallback,kernel-trusted}.test.ts` + `tests/tui/approval.test.ts` (114 pass, incl. a cross-implementation pin). Residuals: grants/leases are in-memory (a restart loses them — the next check asks, fail-closed); the durable CAS lands with the effect host (NF-016); owner-less legacy grants stay globally consultable (as owner-less leases do, ruling 29). Same wave, found by this verification: `ops/gates.sh`'s kernel-selftest gate now ALWAYS rebuilds `kernel/target/release/hz-kernel` — the TS interop suite resolves that path first, so a stale binary had been silently validating old kernel behaviour (the first interop run of this wave passed against a kernel without consumption).
33. **Kernel-mode provenance — the kernel-absent posture, ruled (wave 14).** Coming up without the Rust kernel binary keeps FULL decision functionality: `KernelClient` falls back to the in-process TS kernel and the two implementations are decision-identical (pinned by `app/tests/core/interop.test.ts`, which drives both transports through identical sequences). Fail-closed was rejected: it would trade an interop-verified capability for a posture that constrains nothing above the kernel; the honest control is making the mode first-class. `session.start` payloads now carry `kernelMode: "trusted" | "untrusted"` (read after `kernel.start()`, so the log records what actually ran), an untrusted startup emits exactly one notice naming the mode and the weaker guarantees (OS-level process supervision, file locking, the supervised-kill ladder), later transitions keep the existing onModeChange note path, and no decision behaviour changed. Residuals: the weaker guarantees are real (owned by NF-016); a mid-session mode flip is recorded by the mode-change note, not per event; `tests/tui/headless.test.ts` pins exact stdout and therefore expects the Rust binary to be present (the untrusted notice would add a line) — recorded as the known coupling. Tests: `app/tests/core/kernel-mode-provenance.test.ts` (4).
34. **MCP client v1 (docs/09 §4.7 reduced form; wave 14).** Stdio JSON-RPC spawned through `spawnOwned` only; handshake `initialize` → `notifications/initialized` → `tools/list`; per-server concurrency 2 (excess queued); connect timeout 10 s, call timeout 30 s; transport death → up to 3 reconnects (250 ms·2ⁿ) then marked down with a typed error + notice; `stop()` reaps every child through the spawn ledger. `mcp.invoke` is a guard kind in both kernels (interop-pinned to identical decisions). Tools are `mcp__<serverId>__<toolName>`, parameters are the server's `inputSchema` verbatim, the description carries the untrusted fence (server text is data), `readOnlyHint` absent/false ⇒ mutating, results are fenced as data, and a mid-call crash fails that call typed and unregisters the server's tools. v1 was user-config-only at wave 14; **amendment 2026-11-12:** trusted workspace `mcp.json` / `providers.json` may merge when digests match (rulings 105/113). Malformed entries are skipped with a notice, never a crash. MCP OAuth/HTTP/resources/server mode remain open.
35. **Hooks v1 (wave 14; ratified reference `docs/15` H1–H10 / §3.4 `HookV1`).** The reduced form honours deny-only, deciding-point failure = deny, observation failure = notice, first-deny short-circuit; the named differences (in the module header): `hooks.json` instead of `hooks/hooks.json`, JSON-line protocol instead of exit codes, no `ask` (unclassified ⇒ deny), no argument rewriting, no receipts. A malformed config file is FAIL-CLOSED: every pre call denies naming the file and the parse error (a disabled deny-hook must never silently weaken the configured posture). Hooks run through `spawnOwned` (cwd = workspace, credential-scrubbed env), default timeout 5 s, hard max 300 s, stdout capped 64 KiB, post excerpt capped 4096 chars. **Amendment (2026-11-12 honesty pass):** the wave-14 residual “no trust gate” is **superseded** by rulings **102–113** (`app/src/core/trust.ts` digest bind for hooks/skills/agents/MCP/providers). Remaining residuals: Grok `trust.rs` parity; hook runs are ledger rows, not receipted effects; post hooks run sequentially.

   **Lifecycle superset (the diag-hooks wave; FOLLOWS `docs/15` §2.4 H1, no parallel taxonomy).** The point set is broadened from `tool.pre`/`tool.post` toward the ratified 16-point H1 taxonomy. Now wired, each mapped 1:1 to a ratified H1 point (the `hooks.json` config key is the H1 point in this module's camelCase): `preTool`->`tool.pre` (the only DECIDING point, H3), `postTool`->`tool.post`, `sessionStart`/`sessionEnd`->`session.start`/`session.end` (emitted at the runtime seam, `index.ts`), `turnStart`/`turnEnd`->`step.start`/`step.end` (the turn IS the step a hook observes in v1; emitted once per turn in `loop.ts`), `compactPre`/`compactPost`->`compact.pre`/`compact.post` (bound to the one-report-per-run compaction guard, so the pair fires exactly once and only when a compaction actually happened), and `subagentStart`/`subagentStop`-> the child `session.start`/`session.end` boundary (`subagent.ts`). Every new point is OBSERVATION-only (H2/H4): a failure is a `hook.failure` event plus a notice and NEVER blocks; only `tool.pre` can deny. The emit points are minimal and additive in `loop.ts`; `index.ts` and `subagent.ts` own their own seams. Still residual (H1 points not yet wired): `prompt.submitted`, `tool.error`, `effect.settled`, `commit.pre`/`commit.post`, `error`, `checkpoint.created`, `session.rewound`, and the finer per-provider-call `step` boundary.

   **H9 context channel (RATIFIED by `docs/15` §2.4 H9: "Hook output injected into the model context MUST be bounded, marked as untrusted content, and carried as a declared context fragment").** Because H9 ratifies it, it is implemented directly (not behind a flag): an OBSERVATION hook (`postTool` and every lifecycle point) MAY return a `context` string note in its one JSON output line. The runner (a) enforces a hard byte bound `MAX_HOOK_CONTEXT_BYTES` (4 KiB), oversize is TRUNCATED with a visible marker (never silently dropped and never a failure: an observation note must not become a block); (b) attributes the note to its engine-set hook label plus source file, an UNFORGEABLE attribution the hook bytes cannot set; (c) SANITIZES the note so hook bytes cannot close the untrusted fence (angle brackets/ampersand escaped) or impersonate a role turn (`User:`/`Assistant:`/`System:`/`Human:`/`Tool:` neutralised with a zero-width guard, wherever they appear: line start OR mid-line after whitespace, so a note that escapes a fence close and then forges a turn on the same line is still defused). `loop.ts` renders the collected fragments as ONE fenced, attributed, untrusted `<hook_context untrusted="true">` block on the system rail for the NEXT provider request, then clears them (each note injected once, never re-sent, never a forgeable user turn). A `context` field at `tool.pre` is REJECTED as a protocol failure (deciding-only; H2, annotate never allow). H8 holds: a note annotates, it never undoes the observed effect.

   **Deny/observe/proceed mapping (JSON-line protocol retained, NOT swapped for Claude Code's exit codes).** At `tool.pre`: `{decision:"deny",reason}` -> BLOCK (reason to model); a failure (spawn/write error, timeout, non-zero exit, missing/extra/garbage/unclassified output, a stray `context` field) -> BLOCK (H3) with the failure text INCLUDING the hook's STDERR TAIL on a non-zero exit carried to the model (the JSON-line analogue of Claude Code's exit-2 "stderr to model"); `{decision:"allow"}` -> PROCEED. At an OBSERVATION point: a clean `allow`/`observe` (optional bounded `context`) -> OBSERVE (any note collected as an attributed fragment); a failure -> recorded plus noticed, step PROCEEDS (H4). A worked example hook ships in the repo's hook format: `app/tests/fixtures/hooks/grep-to-rg-validator.js` denies a `grep` shell command naming `rg` in the reason (the H1 `tool.pre` analogue of Claude Code's `bash_command_validator_example.py`).

   **Still residual after this wave (named, not silently changed):** the `ask` verdict and ARGUMENT REWRITING remain deferred (argument rewriting needs end-to-end re-guarding); the trust gate (H7) and receipted hook runs (H6/NF-066) are unchanged residuals. Contracts: NO `contracts.ts` change, because the hook event shapes live in `hooks.ts` (the `HookPoint` union, `HookContextFragment`, the input-line `event` names), not in the frozen effect/tool contract, so no additive contract entry was needed. Tests: `app/tests/core/hooks.test.ts`, `app/tests/core/hooks-loop.test.ts` and the new `app/tests/core/hooks-lifecycle.test.ts` -> 45 pass across the three files (each new lifecycle event fires at the right time; the deny/observe/proceed mapping incl. stderr-to-model on block; the H9 channel's size bound and non-forgeability; the worked grep->rg validator), each failing on revert of the code it covers.
36. **Durable change journal + crash-safe undo (wave 14).** Per-workspace `<state root>/undo.jsonl`, whole-record O_APPEND appends, tombstones `{consume:id}`; a torn trailing line is skipped AND reported; an unreadable/corrupt file is quarantined to `undo.jsonl.corrupt-<epochMillis>` and never read as empty (the spawns.jsonl conventions). `before` ≤ 256 KB (larger records the hash plus `nonRestorable` and undo refuses typed); ≤ 200 live entries; atomic compaction past ~2 MB folds consumed/evicted rows away. Undo is WORKSPACE-scoped and survives restart: newest live entry across sessions by default, `path:` scoping, the stale-check stays (moved-on file refused typed, entry stays), success appends a tombstone, restore keeps `noteRead`. Residuals: no cross-process lock (a compaction racing another process's append can lose that append; a lost tombstone can resurrect an entry after restart) — kernel file locking is NF-016; an entry whose workspace cannot be resolved stays memory-only.
37. **Plan mode + diff review (wave 14; docs/09 §4.6 reduced form).** In plan mode `write`/`edit` stage instead of applying (in-memory, session-scoped, bounded 100 changes / 1 MB with reported oldest-first eviction); `/changes` renders the staged diffs; `/apply` executes each staged change through the real write tool — read-ledger (the review IS the read), atomic write, change journal — after a stale-snapshot check that KEEPS a file that moved on since staging and says why, and issues the ordinary `tool.execute` receipt best-effort (failure noticed, never fatal to the apply); `/discard` drops the stage. The diff is display evidence, not a patch format (line-based, 3 context lines, hard cap with a named truncation marker, byte-stable; an identical input yields ""), and the TUI preview is that same renderer on cards and approval prompts. Residual: the stage does not survive a restart — a durable plan artifact belongs in the kernel receipt chain and does not exist yet.
38. **Landlock spike (wave 14; NF-016 direction, from measurement).** On this deployment kernel (`4.19.0-gvisor`) Landlock is not available at all — the raw `landlock_create_ruleset` syscall returns ENOSYS, there is no LSM list, there is no bwrap — and the previously recorded corpus fact "unprivileged user namespaces blocked" did NOT reproduce: `unshare -U/-Ur/-Urm/-Uprf` all succeed and only bare `unshare -p` EPERMs for lack of CAP_SYS_ADMIN (record: `docs/audit/landlock-spike-2026-11-08.md`; re-runnable probe `ops/landlock-probe.sh`, exit 0 usable / 3 not). Standing direction: design first the portable reduced form — seccomp + `no_new_privs` + rlimits + network-off (netns) for code_mode/shell children — typed `best_effort` with named residuals (no per-path rules); on gVisor/container deployments record the outer sandbox as the confinement of record (it bounds the whole agent, not one command, and cannot be verified from inside); keep Landlock a probe-gated upgrade path (the spike doc's §6 checklist covers ABI versioning, per-path rules and exec restrictions on a ≥ 5.13 kernel). This does not close NF-016; it fixes its direction and its measurement.
39. **TypeScript typecheck is green and gated (minimal, contract-respecting fixes).** The native TS compiler (`tsc --noEmit`, via `bun run typecheck`) reported 30 top-level errors; all are fixed without loosening the compiler (no tsconfig flag changed - `strict:false` stays as-is). By category: (a) **`Stats` import** - `@types/node` 26 exports `Stats` from `node:fs`, not `node:fs/promises`; `atomic-write.ts`/`write.ts` now take it from `node:fs` as a type-only import while the async fns stay on `node:fs/promises`. (b) **Discriminated-union false-branch narrowing** - the native compiler would not narrow `AtomicWriteOutcome` on a bare `!outcome.ok`, so `atomic-write.ts` exports an `isRefused` type guard used at its five call sites (`index.ts`, `grow.ts`, `edit.ts`, `undo.ts`, `write.ts`); the curator/grow/loop unions (`ProposalValidation`, `ProposalLookup`, `projectChange`/`projectExactEdit`) use the equivalent `x.ok === false`. (c) **`Lease.owner`** - `kernel.ts:mintBoundLease` was typed through the frozen `Lease` contract though owner-scoped leases are real (ruling 29/32; the Rust `Lease` carries `owner: Option<String>`); fixed by typing the function's `grant`/local/return through the EXISTING local `OwnedLease` (`owner?: string`), so the frozen `Lease` interface in `contracts.ts` is untouched - no contract addition was needed. (d) **`OwnedProc` streams** - `ReturnType<typeof Bun.spawn>` types stdout/stderr as `number | ReadableStream`; `spawnOwned` pins them to `pipe`, so `owned-spawn.ts` now narrows `OwnedProc` to `ReadableStream<Uint8Array>` at the one source, resolving all seven `getReader`/stream-argument sites (`grep`, `codemode`×2, `mcp/client`×2, `hooks`, `preview`) with no scattered casts. (e) **`Buffer.from` arity** (`write.ts:isUtf8`) - the `@types/bun`/`@types/node` overload collision is sidestepped by wrapping the bytes as a `Buffer` view over the same backing store (no copy), preserving the exact UTF-8 round-trip semantic. (f) **Dead comparisons** - `signal?.aborted === true` (retry/google, TS2367) is rewritten to the truthy abort guard `signal?.aborted` (abort-then-rethrow behaviour identical), and `PostmortemDetail !== ""` is dropped because the type is a closed set of non-empty string literals (the `typeof === "string"` guard already excludes `undefined`). Enforcement: a `ts-typecheck` gate in `ops/gates.sh` (in the GATES array ahead of `ts-suite`, with `gate_ts_typecheck` running `bun run typecheck` in `app/`, and a header-table row) plus an explicit "Typecheck the app" step before "Run the gate suite" in both workflow files (kept byte-identical). Evidence: `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors; `cd app && bun test` → 881 pass / 11 fail (historical, wave-era); `diff .github/workflows/ci.yml ops/ci/github-actions.yml` empty. Residual, named: the 11 failing tests are the root/uid-0 permission-enforcement set (unreadable/unwritable/read-only chains + keyed-anchor interop) - root bypasses chmod bits, so they cannot pass as root and are NOT regressions (the last non-root run was 892/0); under root the `ts-suite` gate therefore FAILs on exactly those 11, an environment artifact this change did not introduce. **Correction (2026-11-09, ruling 51; measured, not asserted).** A real non-root run (uid 1000 via `setpriv`, writable HOME/CARGO_HOME, a scratch copy) showed only 8 of the 11 are root artifacts. The 3 keyed-anchor interop tests failed for every user: the test helper `runKernelNdjson` spawned the kernel with bun's default env, which does not carry a runtime `process.env` mutation (`setEnv({HZ_ANCHOR_KEY})`), so the Rust kernel ran unkeyed. The helper now passes `env: { ...process.env }`, exactly as `KernelClient` already did. The 8 genuine root artifacts are now skipped by a behavioural probe (`permissionBitsBind` in `app/tests/core/helpers.ts`: can this process read a 0o000 file?) and run wherever permission bits bind. Results: as uid 1000 `bun test` → 1267 pass / 0 fail; as root → 1259 pass / 8 skip / 0 fail (historical, at the 2026-11-09 tree).
40. **Three loop-level safety rails (SAFETY-RAILS, NF-073 extension).** Three self-contained pure guards under `app/src/core/guards/`, wired into `loop.ts` with minimal and unit-testable surface. The modules import nothing from the loop; the loop constructs one of each per run. Two additive terminal `RunExit.detail` reasons were added to `contracts.ts`, both terminal `failed` so `exitCodeFor` returns 1 through its default arm (no change to `exitCodeFor`): `tool_loop` and `consecutive_mistakes`. (a) **Loop detection** (`loop-detection.ts`, ported from cline). It counts consecutive identical tool calls, where identity is name plus a canonical signature of the parsed arguments (object keys sorted with the JavaScript default string order, deliberately NOT the `contracts.ts` UTF-8 byte order, because this rail only compares a call against the one before it and never hashes for interop). Before a batch is dispatched the loop inspects each call: the 3rd identical call is a soft nudge, verbatim "Detected N consecutive identical calls to `<tool>`; consider trying a different approach.", which rides the next step's system rail on its own channel so the format corrective reset cannot clear it; the 5th identical call is a hard stop, verbatim "Detected N consecutive identical calls to `<tool>`; stopping to avoid a loop.", which ends the run with `tool_loop` before that batch runs. (b) **Mistake tracker** (`mistake-tracker.ts`, ported from cline), a NEW axis that is kept SEPARATE from the NF-073 format cap and never folded into it. It counts consecutive mistakes across three reasons, `api_error` (a provider error), `invalid_tool_call` (a pure-format-error batch, recorded in ADDITION to the format cap, which keeps priority and its own `format_errors` detail) and `tool_execution_failed` (a tool that returned `ok:false`, excluding plan-mode staged calls and calls whose arguments did not parse so neither axis double-counts a batch). A clean successful tool step resets it. At the default limit of 3 (a module constant distinct from `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FORMAT_ERRORS`) it stops the run with `consecutive_mistakes` and a message ending verbatim with "Session state was preserved. Send a new prompt to resume from the latest state." (c) **Repeat-tool reminder** (`repeat-tool.ts`, ported from deepseek-harness), an ADVISORY guard that never vetoes. Thresholds default to [3, 5, 8] and are validated fail-loud (non-empty, every value an integer at least 2, no duplicates, returned sorted ascending). It keeps one consecutive chain keyed by the tool name and the deep-canonical arguments; `observe` is called POST-execute in deterministic order so a denied or failed call still advances the chain, and `clear` runs for each new user prompt. When a count hits the first threshold it returns the gentle reminder, otherwise the detailed reminder (whose quoted arguments are head-truncated to `argumentsPreviewChars`, default 500, while the chain key always uses the full canonical string); the loop injects the text as a user message prefixed `[repeat-tool-reminder]`. Include and exclude are `*`-wildcard predicates; an untracked tool is transparent and neither counts nor resets the chain. `classifyFailure` maps both new details to the existing coarse `unknown` class (the `FailureClass` set in `postmortem.ts` is a closed set this change does not own; the exit terminal plus detail already carry the precise outcome). Evidence: `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors; `cd app && bun test tests/core/guards tests/core/rails.test.ts tests/core/rails-guards.test.ts` → all pass; `cd app && bun test` → no new failures versus the 881 pass / 11 fail baseline (the 11 are the root-only permission artifacts of ruling 39).
41. **Edit-engine superset — CRLF safety, the unicode-fold rung, named thresholds, richer miss feedback (FEAT-001/FEAT-002, feat/edit-engine-superset).** (Ruling numbered 41 on the integration branch; the branch itself numbered it 40 and the plan named 39, but 39 is the typecheck gate and 40 is the safety rails; renumbered so the sequence stays one ascending run.) **(FEAT-001, CRLF safety.)** Every rung now matches against an LF *view* of the file (`before.replaceAll("\r\n","\n")`), the same bytes `read` shows the model; the exact path maps matched view offsets back to the original bytes through the ascending list of removed-`\r` positions (`toLfView`/`viewToSource`), and the fuzzy path carries each original line's native ending beside the LF-view line (`fuzzyModel`/`spliceWithEndings`). On write, untouched regions keep their exact bytes and the replaced block takes the matched region's detected ending, so a CRLF (or mixed-ending) file is NEVER flattened to LF wholesale; a pure-LF file is byte-identical to the pre-superset behaviour. The shared `app/src/tools/line-endings.ts` (`detectLineEnding` — CRLF if `\r\n` appears anywhere, else LF — and `normalizeLineEndings`) is dependency-free (no `os.EOL` gating) and used by `write` for new-file / overwrite normalisation. An explicit endings-only escape is intentionally out of scope (documented in the module header: with LF-only old/new strings nothing requests a bare ending flip). **(FEAT-002, ladder + feedback.)** A new `unicode-fold` rung sits in `EDIT_LADDER` between `indent-normalised` and `3-way merge` — a byte-level normalisation (`normalizeForMatch`) folding EXACTLY codex's `seek_sequence.rs` code points: smart single quotes `\u2018\u2019\u201A\u201B` → `'`, smart double quotes `\u201C\u201D\u201E\u201F` → `"`, typographic dashes `\u2010\u2011\u2012\u2013\u2014\u2015\u2212` → `-`, and the space family `\u00A0\u2002\u2003\u2004\u2005\u2006\u2007\u2008\u2009\u200A\u202F\u205F\u3000` → ` `. It runs under the same `windowsMatching` unique-match machinery as every fuzzy rung: it applies only on a UNIQUE fold match, only after the read-before-write gate (`wasRead`), and only while the reflection slot is unspent — exact still beats fold, fold still beats the looser trim/anchor rungs, and more than one folded window refuses with the named rung + candidate spans and no write. The named oh-my-pi thresholds (`DEFAULT_FUZZY_THRESHOLD=0.95`, `SEQUENCE_FUZZY_THRESHOLD=0.92`, `FALLBACK_THRESHOLD=0.8`, `CONTEXT_FUZZY_THRESHOLD=0.8`, `CHARACTER_MATCH_THRESHOLD=0.92`, `DOMINANT_FUZZY_MIN_CONFIDENCE=0.97`, `DOMINANT_FUZZY_DELTA=0.08`, `PARTIAL_MATCH_MIN_LENGTH=6`, `PARTIAL_MATCH_MIN_RATIO=0.3`) are exported module consts recorded as a documented, future-stable PRIOR — the ladder stays STRUCTURAL (a rung matches on equal + unique windows, never on a similarity score), so these similarity/confidence constants are deliberately NOT wired into a scoring path (doing so would change which matches apply and break the unique-match discipline). The feedback consts (`MAX_RECORDED_MATCHES=5`, `OCCURRENCE_PREVIEW_CONTEXT=5`, `OCCURRENCE_PREVIEW_MAX_LEN=80`) ARE wired: miss feedback now adds, additively after the existing first diff-shaped `closestBlock`, up to 5 closest-candidate previews (5 context lines each, every line truncated to 80 chars with `\u2026`), and the already-present (“may already be applied”) note and all content-revealing lines are gated on `wasRead` — mirroring opencode's read-permission gate on missHint. The A-5 contract fixtures in `tests/tools/edit-ladder.test.ts` were updated in LOCKSTEP for exactly the strings that legitimately changed: the three `searched: …` lines now read `exact \u2192 indent-normalised \u2192 unicode-fold \u2192 3-way merge \u2192 line-patch` (driven by `EDIT_LADDER.join`), and the closest-lines diff test gains the additive `closest candidates (up to 5, 5 lines each):` preview block; no other pinned string drifted. Evidence: `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors; `bun test tests/tools/edit-superset.test.ts tests/tools/edit-ladder.test.ts tests/tools/edit-crlf.test.ts tests/tools/edit.test.ts tests/tools/line-endings.test.ts` all pass; full `bun test` 874 pass / 13 fail (the 13 are the root-only permission + Rust-kernel-absent artifacts of this fresh worktree, unchanged — +8 passing = exactly the new `edit-superset` tests). New/changed tests FAIL on revert of the fold rung, the ladder order, the preview bounds, or the read gate. Residuals: the similarity thresholds are a named prior awaiting a score-based rung (none is fabricated now); the endings-only escape stays out of scope; contracts.ts is untouched (no new tool, no new ToolContext field).
42. **Command allowlist for `shell.exec` (FEAT-002; NF-016 item 11, PARTIALLY ADDRESSED).** A declarative command allowlist modeled on codex execpolicy, in the new pure-logic module `kernel/src/execpolicy.rs`. Shapes: `Decision { Allow, Ask, Deny }` with `Ord` = `Deny > Ask > Allow` (so `from_matches` = max = most-restrictive-wins, the docs/11 R1 spirit); `PatternToken { Single(String), Alts(Vec<String>) }`; `PrefixPattern { canonical_matching, first, rest }` matching a parsed argv per-token (first token keyed, each rest token `Single`==exact or `Alts` contains) — stronger than `guard.rs::pattern_matches` (a flat trailing-`*`), so `git commit` is not satisfied by `git commitfoo`; `PrefixRule` wraps the pattern + a `Decision` + `match`/`notMatch` examples that are validated at load and refused if they fail (codex R8); `NetworkRule { host, protocol, decision, justification }` with `normalize_network_rule_host` (strip scheme/path/port/userinfo, reject wildcards + whitespace, lowercase, reject empty); `ExecPolicy { rules_by_program: BTreeMap<String, Vec<PrefixRule>>, network_rules }`. `fingerprint()` is order-independent: the sorted `"{program}:{rule_debug}"` strings (incl. network rules) wrapped in a JSON array and hashed with `canon::canonical_json` + `canon::sha256_hex` — LAW reused, NO new hash scheme; two policies built in different orders fingerprint equal. Load reads `<state-dir>/allowlist.json` (a bare array or `{rules, networkRules}`) and FAILS CLOSED on a malformed file as a typed `HZ_PRECONDITION_FAILED` refusal naming the bad rule index (mirrors `read_policy_rules`); a genuinely absent file is `None` ("no allowlist opinion"). Runtime amend (`amend_allow_prefix_rule` / `amend_network_rule`) is append-dedup under an `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` advisory lockfile with a bounded retry + stale-lock clear, rewriting `allowlist.json` canonical at 0600 via `permm::write_new`. Wiring: `guard.rs::check` consults the allowlist for `shell.exec` ONLY, BEFORE the policy rules — an allowlist `Deny` returns deny (R1, reason citing the fingerprint) before any policy rule, an allowlist `Allow` admits, and `None`/`Ask` fall through to the existing policy/lease/grant/ask flow unchanged, so `shell.exec` with NO allowlist file present behaves EXACTLY as today (ask). New protocol method `guard.allowlist.amend` (additive, unknown `kind` refused like `guard.lease`; returns `{changed, fingerprint}`), mirrored in `app/src/contracts.ts` `KERNEL_METHODS` + `KernelRequestMap` with the `AllowlistPrefixRule`/`AllowlistNetworkRule` shapes. NO `GuardDecision` wire field was added — the decision still carries only `{decision, reason, leaseId?}` and the NDJSON envelope is unchanged; the allowlist surfaces through the existing `reason` string (justified in the contracts.ts comment). Residuals, named: the argv splitter is the reduced form (whitespace tokenization) — wrapper unwrapping (`env`/`timeout`/…), quote handling and a tree-sitter per-segment split are DEFERRED (a conservative split never widens authority: a non-match falls through to ask); `canonical_key` is simple Unicode lowercasing (not full NFD + case-fold) to avoid a unicode-normalization dependency, documented in the module header and shared by the index and the matcher so they never disagree; amend is advisory-locked (not a kernel lease). **TESTED** (TESTED, not VERIFIED beyond the commands below): `cd kernel && CARGO_HOME=/tmp/cargo-home cargo test --locked` → 19 unit pass + 1 known root artifact (`eventlog::tests::warm_cache_still_refuses_a_rewritten_tail`, ruling 39), 40 integration pass (was 35: +`allowlist_deny_wins_before_policy_for_shell_exec`, `allowlist_allow_admits_shell_exec`, `absent_allowlist_leaves_shell_exec_asking`, `malformed_allowlist_is_a_typed_refusal`, `allowlist_amend_appends_and_takes_effect`); new unit tests in `execpolicy.rs` (`canonical_key_is_caseless`, `pattern_token_alts_match_any`, `prefix_match_allows_extra_trailing_args_but_not_shorter`, `most_restrictive_wins_aggregation`, `fingerprint_is_order_independent`, `normalize_host_accepts_and_rejects`, `malformed_allowlist_fails_closed`, `absent_allowlist_is_none_not_error`, `example_validation_refuses_a_bad_prefix_rule`, `amend_appends_dedups_and_reload_sees_it`, `fingerprint_stable_across_amend_reorder`); `./target/release/hz-kernel --selftest --state-dir $(mktemp -d)` → SELFTEST OK (21 checks, was 18: +absent-asks, +amend-appends, +allowlist-deny-wins); contracts.ts touched → `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors, `bun test` → 881 pass / 11 fail (the known root/uid-0 permission set, ruling 39; last non-root run 892/0 — zero NEW failures). Does NOT close NF-016 (no OS confinement): this is admission-time policy, the kernel-executed effect host remains the class fix.
43. **Per-origin egress decider for `net.fetch` (FEAT-003; NF-016 item 11 / docs/11 N1–N10, PARTIALLY ADDRESSED — decider + policy landed, the routed MITM proxy still open).** A per-origin network-authority decider modeled on grok's egress proxy (`research/compare-codex-grok.md` §2.1), in the new pure-logic module `kernel/src/egress.rs`.
