Shapes, byte-identical to `app/src/contracts.ts`: `Origin { scheme, host, port }` (docs/11 N2 — a destination is a `(scheme, host, port)` triple, not a boolean) with `parse_origin` reusing the `execpolicy::normalize_network_rule_host` style (lowercase host, strip userinfo/path, reject wildcards + whitespace, default ports 443/80 per scheme) and FAILING CLOSED on an unparseable url (a deny candidate, never Allow); `EgressDecision { Allow, Deny(DenySource), Ask, AllowAndRecord { would: WouldBe } }`; `WouldBe { Allowed, Denied(DenySource), Asked }`; `DenySource { DenyRow, WebFetchDenylist }` (a per-host deny row vs the org `disallowedWebFetchDomains` list); `OriginRule { host, scheme?, port?, decision }` and `EgressPolicy { rules, disallowed_web_fetch_domains, observe }`. The decider is DEFAULT-DENY (docs/11 N1): precedence is org-denylist → a matching deny row (most-restrictive-wins, R1 spirit) → a matching allow → a matching ask → **default deny** when nothing matched. Observe mode (the `observe` flag) wraps every enforce-mode answer into `AllowAndRecord{would}` — it ADMITS and records what enforce mode WOULD have done, blocking nothing (grok's safe-rollout path). The two-level hold budget is modeled as PURE data: `HoldBudget` with `DEFAULT_MAX_HELD=16` proxy-wide and `DEFAULT_MAX_HELD_PER_CALL=8` per `CommandTag`, `try_hold(tag) → Result<HoldToken, Overloaded>` refusing over-budget (`Overloaded::ProxyWide`/`PerCall`, grok's "503 overloaded before a card"), `release(token)`, `DEFAULT_HOLD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600` and a `deadline_unix` computation. Records are CONTENT-FREE: `BlockedRequest { host, port, call, decided, hold_ms, deadline_unix }` with NO path/headers/body (a unit test pins the JSON keys and the absence of `path`/`headers`/`body`/`url`/`query`), bounded to `BLOCKED_REQUEST_CAPACITY=256`; `to_json`/`to_canonical_json` go through `canon::canonical_json` — LAW reused, NO new hash scheme. Load reads `<state-dir>/egress.json` (a bare array of rules, or `{rules, disallowedWebFetchDomains, observe}`) and FAILS CLOSED on a malformed file as a typed `HZ_PRECONDITION_FAILED` refusal naming the bad rule index (mirrors `execpolicy::load` / `read_policy_rules`); a genuinely absent file is `None`. Wiring: `guard.rs::check` consults the decider for `net.fetch` ONLY, BEFORE the policy rules — a `Deny` returns deny (reason naming the origin + `DenySource`) before any policy rule, an `Allow` admits, and `Ask`/`AllowAndRecord` fall through to the existing policy/lease/grant/ask flow unchanged. **Composition, documented so it is unambiguous:** the decider is consulted only when `egress.json` is PRESENT — a genuinely absent file leaves `net.fetch` at its policy-layer `ask`, BYTE-IDENTICAL to today (the `DEFAULT_POLICY net.fetch=ask` line is unchanged); once a file exists it is default-deny (N1), so an unmatched origin denies. That two-layer split is deliberate: absence of config = ask (the shipped behaviour), presence of config = deny-by-default (the confined profile N1 demands). No new NDJSON method was added (the decider lives inside `guard.check`); no `GuardDecision` wire field was added — the decision still carries only `{decision, reason, leaseId?}` and the NDJSON envelope is unchanged; the egress outcome surfaces through the existing `reason` string (justified in the contracts.ts comment). Contract additions in `app/src/contracts.ts` are ADDITIVE: `EgressOrigin`, `EgressDenySource`, `EgressWouldBe`, `EgressDecision`, `EgressOutcome`, `EgressBaseDecision`, `EgressOriginRule`, `EgressPolicyDoc`, `EgressBlockedRequest`, and the `EGRESS_DEFAULT_MAX_HELD`/`EGRESS_DEFAULT_MAX_HELD_PER_CALL`/`EGRESS_DEFAULT_HOLD_TIMEOUT_SECS`/`EGRESS_BLOCKED_REQUEST_CAPACITY` constants — each with a justifying comment; `KERNEL_METHODS`/`KernelRequestMap` and `DEFAULT_POLICY` are untouched. **Residuals, named and DEFERRED:** the real connection parking (grok's `hold()` → `oneshot::Receiver<bool>`), the async MITM/routed-proxy that enforces N10 (a sandbox destination MUST route through the local proxy), and DNS pinning / rebind re-check (N4) are OUT of scope — the `HoldBudget`/`BlockedRequest` here are the accounting-and-data half only (no connection is actually parked), carried under a documented module-level `allow(dead_code)` until that proxy lands; spawn-with-network intent is NOT cleanly derivable from a spawn target, so this consult is scoped to `net.fetch` and spawn-with-network is a documented follow-up; N3/N5/N6/N7/N9 (https-only default, no-redirect, request bounds, literal-address approval, content-addressed fetch) belong to that enforcing proxy and remain open. **TESTED** (TESTED, not VERIFIED beyond the commands below): `cd kernel && CARGO_HOME=/tmp/cargo-home cargo test --locked` → 35 unit pass + 1 known root artifact (`eventlog::tests::warm_cache_still_refuses_a_rewritten_tail`, ruling 39), was 19 unit (+16 egress unit tests: `parse_origin_lowercases_host_and_applies_default_ports`, `parse_origin_fails_closed_on_garbage`, `default_deny_when_no_rule_matches`, `per_origin_rule_returns_its_decision`, `deny_row_wins_over_allow_row_most_restrictive`, `web_fetch_denylist_denies_with_its_source`, `scheme_and_port_scoped_rules_match_exactly`, `observe_mode_records_would_without_blocking`, `decide_url_fails_closed_on_unparseable`, `hold_budget_refuses_at_the_proxy_wide_ceiling`, `hold_budget_refuses_at_the_per_call_ceiling`, `deadline_unix_adds_the_hold_timeout`, `blocked_request_is_content_free`, `malformed_egress_policy_fails_closed`, `absent_egress_policy_is_none_not_error`, `object_form_parses_rules_denylist_and_observe`); 46 integration pass (was 40: +`egress_deny_rule_denies_net_fetch_before_policy`, `egress_default_deny_for_unmatched_origin_when_policy_present`, `egress_allow_rule_admits_net_fetch`, `absent_egress_policy_leaves_net_fetch_asking`, `egress_observe_mode_admits_without_blocking`, `malformed_egress_policy_is_a_typed_refusal`); `./target/release/hz-kernel --selftest --state-dir $(mktemp -d)` → SELFTEST OK (21 checks, unchanged — selftest not extended); contracts.ts touched → `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors, `bun test` → 881 pass / 11 fail (the known root/uid-0 permission set, ruling 39; zero NEW failures). Does NOT close NF-016 (no OS confinement) and does NOT fully satisfy docs/11 N1–N10: this is the admission-time decider + policy (N1/N2 for `net.fetch`), the routed MITM proxy and OS network-off confinement remain the class fix.
44. **Fail-closed `SandboxMode` ladder with capability detection (FEAT-004; NF-016 item 11 / docs/11 S1–S9, PARTIALLY ADDRESSED — the ladder + markers + `approveEscalation` + enforcement-completeness land regardless of kernel LSM availability; the OS enforcement backend is capability-detected and FAILS CLOSED).** A per-call fail-closed sandbox ladder modeled on dsh's `escalation.ts` (`research/compare-ohmypi-deepseek.md` §2.4, L140–146), in the new module `kernel/src/sandbox.rs`. **Two layers.** (1) The PURE ladder/contract: `SandboxMode { ReadOnly, WorkspaceWrite, DangerFullAccess }` with stable wire strings `"read-only"`/`"workspace-write"`/`"danger-full-access"`; the strictly-`WIDER_MODES` table (`read-only → [workspace-write, danger-full-access]`, `workspace-write → [danger-full-access]`, `danger-full-access → []`) checked at execution (`is_strictly_wider`), not in a schema enum; `ESCALATION_TARGETS = [workspace-write, danger-full-access]` (read-only is the floor, nothing escalates TO it); the model-facing markers reproduced BYTE-FOR-BYTE from the dsh source — `sandbox_denial_marker(mode)` → `[sandbox: file access denied under ${mode} mode]` and `escalation_hint_marker(subject)` → `[sandbox: escalation available — retry this exact ${subject} once with sandbox_permissions (the narrowest wider mode that suffices) + justification; the approval prompt asks the user]` (the "available"/"retry" separator is a literal EM DASH U+2014, pinned by a unit test); `validate_escalation_args` enforcing that `sandbox_permissions` + `justification` travel together with a non-empty justification; and `approve_escalation(request)` — repeating the effective mode needs no approval, a strictly-wider mode requires an approver + a grant and applies to THAT ONE call only, and a narrower/equal-but-claimed-wider target, an unsupported target, a missing approver/agent, or a non-grant outcome all THROW before execution (pure, deterministic, fully unit-tested). (2) The capability-detected OS layer: `EnforcementCompleteness { Full, Partial }`, a per-dimension guarantee vector `DimensionReport { dimension, guarantee: enforced|best_effort|unsupported, backend (explicit name or "none", docs/11 S5), residual (NON-EMPTY whenever below enforced, docs/11 S2/S3) }`, an `EnforcementReport { mode, completeness, backend, dimensions, unavailable_reason? }`, and an `EnforcementOutcome::{ Report, Unavailable { mode, reason } }` where `Unavailable` is the typed `SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE` (HZ_PRECONDITION_FAILED class). **Capability detection is RUNTIME, not hardcoded** (cached once via `OnceLock`, each detector fail-closed to unsupported on ANY error, NEVER panics), using std + a raw syscall / `/proc` inspection and NO new dependency (`--locked` intact): Landlock FS is usable ONLY when BOTH the raw `landlock_create_ruleset(NULL, 0, LANDLOCK_CREATE_RULESET_VERSION)` syscall (x86_64 inline-asm syscall 444; off-x86_64 fails closed) returns a usable ABI version AND `landlock` is in the active LSM list (`/sys/kernel/security/lsm`) — a present syscall with NO active LSM is still unsupported; `bwrap` is detected by presence on PATH but presence ALONE is NOT FS enforcement (without an FS-LSM backing it cannot provide kernel-enforced per-path confinement); unprivileged userns via `/proc/sys/kernel/unprivileged_userns_clone` or `max_user_namespaces`; seccomp via the `Seccomp:` line in `/proc/self/status`; rlimits via `/proc/self/limits`; netns via `/proc/self/ns/net` + userns. **Live detection on this build host (Linux 6.1.189-amzn2023, uid 0) recorded:** `landlock_create_ruleset` symbol present in `/proc/kallsyms` BUT `/sys/kernel/security/lsm` ABSENT ⇒ Landlock FS **unsupported**; `bwrap` present at `/usr/bin/bwrap` (bubblewrap 0.12.0) BUT treated as best_effort/unsupported for FS (no LSM backing); unprivileged userns **works**; seccomp, rlimits, netns **available**. This is a VARIANCE from the gVisor spike (`4.19.0-gvisor`: Landlock ENOSYS, no LSM, no bwrap) yet the SAME fail-closed verdict for FS on both classes. **The fail-closed choice, explicit:** `read-only` and `workspace-write` REQUIRE kernel-enforced per-path FS confinement, which is unavailable on both host classes, so a request for either mode yields `SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE` by default ("fail when the mode requires the missing dimension", docs/11 S1/S4, dsh SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE) — NEVER a silent `enforced`, NEVER silently unconfined; a caller that can tolerate a partial boundary passes `allowPartial` to instead receive a `completeness=partial` report with the FS dimension `unsupported` + a non-empty residual + backend `none` (S2/S5), while the mechanisms that DO work (seccomp/netns `enforced`, rlimits `best_effort`) are reported with evidence [SUPERSEDED, see the correction at the end of this ruling: nothing is applied, so nothing is `enforced`]; `danger-full-access` reports NO dimension enforced (S4). **Exposure:** an additive NDJSON method `sandbox.report` (validates the mode + the optional escalation pairing, fail-closed) returns the `EnforcementReport`/`SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE`; the NDJSON envelope and every existing shape are unchanged, and NO `GuardDecision` wire field was added (the frozen `{decision, reason, leaseId?}` is untouched — the one-shot widen that would attach per-call metadata is a documented DEFERRED runtime-seam follow-up, carried under a module-level `allow(dead_code)` exactly like `egress.rs`). Contract additions in `app/src/contracts.ts` are ADDITIVE and byte-identical in semantics, each with a justifying comment: `SandboxMode`, `SANDBOX_WIDER_MODES`, `SANDBOX_ESCALATION_TARGETS`, `sandboxDenialMarker`, `escalationHintMarker` (EM DASH U+2014 preserved), `SandboxEscalationArgs`, `EnforcementCompleteness`, `SandboxGuarantee`, `SandboxDimensionReport`, `EnforcementReport`, `SandboxUnavailable`, `EnforcementOutcome`, plus the `sandbox.report` entry in `KERNEL_METHODS` + `KernelRequestMap`; `DEFAULT_POLICY` is untouched. **Residuals, named and DEFERRED:** the ladder is the admission-time report + contract, NOT OS confinement — the actual bubblewrap+Landlock/seccomp/netns backend that would make a mode `enforced`, and the runtime seam that applies a one-shot `approve_escalation` widen (the TUI approval prompt + per-call GuardDecision metadata), remain open; FS confinement stays `SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE` on every kernel without an active Landlock LSM; the raw-syscall detector is x86_64-only (other arches fail closed). **TESTED** (TESTED, not VERIFIED beyond the commands below): `cd kernel && CARGO_HOME=/tmp/cargo-home cargo test --locked` → 46 unit pass + 1 known root artifact (`eventlog::tests::warm_cache_still_refuses_a_rewritten_tail`, ruling 39), was 35 unit (+11 sandbox unit tests: `wider_modes_are_strictly_wider`, `markers_are_byte_exact`, `validate_escalation_args_requires_the_pair`, `approve_escalation_same_mode_needs_no_approval`, `approve_escalation_strictly_wider_is_one_shot_with_approval`, `approve_escalation_throws_before_execution`, `capability_detection_never_panics`, `fs_confined_modes_fail_closed_here`, `fs_confined_partial_report_has_nonempty_residual_and_explicit_backend`, `danger_full_access_reports_no_enforced_dimension`, `outcome_json_is_stable_and_typed`); 50 integration pass (was 46: +`sandbox_report_fs_mode_is_unavailable_not_silently_enforced`, `sandbox_report_partial_has_nonempty_residual_and_explicit_backend`, `sandbox_report_danger_full_access_has_no_enforced_dimension`, `sandbox_report_validates_escalation_args_over_the_protocol`); `./target/release/hz-kernel --selftest --state-dir $(mktemp -d)` → SELFTEST OK (25 checks, was 21: +read-only SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE, +workspace-write partial FS-unsupported, +danger-full-access no-enforced-dimension, +lone-sandbox_permissions refusal); contracts.ts touched → `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors, `bun test` → 881 pass / 11 fail (the known root/uid-0 permission set, ruling 39; zero NEW failures — the 4 interop failures among them are the keyed-anchor/unreadable-chain root artifacts, confirmed present on the base state too). Does NOT close NF-016: the kernel reports the fail-closed posture honestly; the OS confinement backend remains the class fix. **Correction (2026-11-09, hosted CI on `72ad830`; ruling 51).** The ladder as first landed was NOT honest on two counts. (1) Overclaim: `full_confined_report` reported filesystem `enforced` / backend `landlock` whenever Landlock was DETECTED, and `network_dimension`/`process_dimension` reported `enforced` via `netns`/`seccomp` whenever those were detected, but no code in the kernel or the app ever applies a Landlock ruleset, seccomp filter, netns or rlimit to a spawned process (grep: no `landlock_restrict_self`, `seccomp`, `unshare`, `CLONE_NEW*`, `setrlimit`/`prlimit`, `pre_exec` outside detection). That violated docs/11 S3 (an unimplemented dimension MUST be `unsupported`). The partial report on THIS dev host already claimed network `enforced` (netns) and process `enforced` (seccomp); on the Landlock-capable hosted runner `sandbox.report` also claimed filesystem `enforced`. (2) Host-dependent tests: `fs_confined_modes_fail_closed_here` and three siblings asserted the live host's capabilities, so they passed on the no-Landlock dev sandbox and failed on the runner (43 pass / 4 fail). **Fix:** `enforce(mode, caps, applied, demand_full)` now takes both the host capabilities and a new `AppliedMechanisms` (what the kernel actually applies) as parameters; the live probe (`capabilities()`) and `kernel_applied_mechanisms()` (all-false today, pinned by `kernel_applies_no_mechanism_today`) are supplied only at the outermost call (`protocol.rs` `sandbox.report`). A dimension is `enforced` ONLY when available AND applied. Today every FS-confining mode yields `SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE` on every host; the reason (and the partial report's FS residual) says when Landlock IS available but not applied. Every dimension of a partial report is `unsupported`, backend `none`, with a residual that distinguishes "available on this host but this kernel does not apply it yet" from "unavailable on this host". Detection stays and is reported ADDITIVELY: `available: bool` on each dimension and `hostCapabilities {landlockFs, bwrapPresent, userns, seccomp, rlimits, netns}` on both outcomes (the meaning of every existing field is unchanged; `contracts.ts` mirrors both as optional fields). `danger-full-access` is unchanged (no enforced dimension). Tests are now host-independent: synthetic `caps_none()`/`caps_all()` sets drive both the no-Landlock and Landlock-detected cases; `enforced_requires_both_available_and_applied` pins the seam for a future backend. Before the fix, `enforce(ReadOnly, caps_all, demand_full=true)` returned `Report{filesystem: Enforced/landlock, network: Enforced/netns, process: Enforced/seccomp, ...}`; after, `SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE`. `cargo test --locked` → 49 unit + 50 integration pass, identical as root and as uid 1000; selftest 25 checks (the partial-report check now also asserts no dimension is enforced).
45. **Tool exposure classes + `tool_search` (FEAT-002; research §1.6, codex ToolExposure reference).** The `Tool` interface in `app/src/contracts.ts` gains two ADDITIVE optional fields, justified in-comment and recorded here in the same commit: `exposure?: "direct" | "deferred" | "code_mode_only" | "hidden"` (absent === `"direct"`, exactly as absent `concurrencyClass` === `"shared"` - so every shipped-Direct tool is unchanged) and `searchInfo?: { keywords: string[] }` (the extra discovery surface beyond the tool's own name/description). No frozen shape changed: `ToolSpec` is untouched and `tool_search` returns specs in that same shape. The gating lives in `tools/index.ts`: `exposureOf(tool)` defaults to Direct; `toolSpecs(sessionId?)` emits Direct tools plus Deferred tools this session has discovered, and never Hidden / undiscovered-Deferred / CodeModeOnly (MCP specs unchanged); a per-session in-process discovery registry (`discoverDeferredTools`/`isDiscovered`/`deferredSearchCandidates`/`__resetToolDiscoveryForTests`) mirrors the other session maps. The new Direct meta-tool `tool_search` (`app/src/tools/tool-search.ts`) tokenises the query and scores it against each Deferred candidate's name + description + keywords (case-insensitive, ≥3-char substring/token match so common stop-words like "a"/"an" never spuriously match - data-driven, no per-tool-id branching), unlocks the matched tools for the rest of the session, and returns their full specs so the model can call them immediately; a no-match query discovers nothing. `preview` is the Deferred tool chosen (a niche dev-server tool; keywords preview/dev server/serve/localhost/http/port) - no production test assumed preview was always in the spec list, so no test-only fixture was needed (the one test that iterates the whole registry, `authority-response.test.ts`, gained a `tool_search` sample args entry, and `tool_search` admits as session-state like `todo`/`preview`/`undo`). `loop.ts` builds specs via `toolSpecs(deps.sessionId)` and adds a defense-in-depth dispatch refusal: a resolved tool that is Hidden, or Deferred-and-undiscovered for the session, or CodeModeOnly, is refused with the same shape as the unknown/filtered path (`getTool` still resolves any registered tool - exposure gates the SPECS the model sees, not dispatch resolution). Residual, named: `code_mode_only` is reserved - it is in the enum and excluded from the default specs, but its code-mode child-list wiring is deliberately not added (codemode.ts is not edited); only Direct/Deferred/Hidden are fully wired. No kernel event: the reserved `hz.tool.discovered` name (docs/_conventions.md §6) is NOT promoted - discovery is runtime-only (contracts.ts + registry + loop). Tests: `app/tests/tools/tool-exposure.test.ts` (8) - default specs are the Direct builtins with preview absent; tool_search discovers preview, which then rides `toolSpecs(sessionId)`, resolves via getTool and is session-scoped; the spec is returned in the output; Hidden/CodeModeOnly never appear; a no-keyword query discovers nothing; candidates are data-driven. Evidence: `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors; `bun test tests/tools/tool-exposure.test.ts` → 8 pass; full suite (historical, wave-era) 851 pass / 13 fail (the 13 are the pre-existing root/uid-0 permission artifacts - ruling 39 - not regressions).
46. **Turn-diff surface (FEAT-003; research §1.11/§3, codex `turn_diff_tracker.rs` reference).** A single model turn can write/edit several files; where a `tool.card` shows one effect at a time, this surface aggregates the WHOLE turn into ONE unified diff. The event is an ADDITIVE `RuntimeEvent` variant on the harness-side union in `app/src/contracts.ts`: `{ type: "turn.diff"; turn: number; files: number; diff: string; truncated?: boolean }`, justified in-comment as a computed UI/runtime FACADE event - the same kind of surface as the existing `context.usage` and `turn.done` variants, which are emitted for the TUI and are never written to the session log, never projected, and carry no seq/hash chain. Because it is a `RuntimeEvent` and NOT a kernel canonical event, the CLOSED 44-name kernel catalogue (`docs/_conventions.md` §6) is UNCHANGED; `docs/06-events-and-flows.md` §4.2 records that facade events live outside that catalogue, and `turn.diff` is explicitly not a new canonical event (the reserved `hz.tool.discovered` name is unrelated and stays reserved). The aggregator is a small new module `app/src/core/turn-diff.ts` (`computeTurnDiff(entries, maxLines?)`): given the change-journal entries recorded DURING one turn it emits one net diff per file - `before` from the EARLIEST entry for that file (`null` = new file), `after` the CURRENT on-disk content read once at turn end - collapsing multiple edits to the same file into a single net diff, REUSING `unifiedDiff` from `core/plan.ts` (byte-stable, 3-line context; no new diff algorithm). `nonRestorable`/`beforeUnreadable` preimages are noted inline (diff-from-empty) rather than crashing, a disk read that fails (a file deleted after its edit) is a safe empty `after`, files are emitted in first-change order (byte-stable), and a total line cap sets `truncated`. `change-journal.ts` gains ONE ADDITIVE export `liveEntries(sessionId)` - a fresh copy of the already-merged live view `lastChange`/`lastChangeFor` read; no existing signature or behaviour changed. `loop.ts` is minimal and additive: just before the turn's `batchSegments` tool batch it snapshots the session's journal entry-id boundary, and after the batch it diffs the entries appended since that boundary and emits exactly one `turn.diff` via `emit(deps.callbacks, …)` - ONLY when `files > 0`. A read-only turn emits none; a plan-mode turn stages writes WITHOUT recording to the journal, so it produces no new entries and correctly emits no `turn.diff`. The emit is best-effort (wrapped so a failure never breaks the run). `tui/render.ts` is unchanged: its `default` arm already no-ops unknown RuntimeEvents (as it does for `context.usage`), so typecheck stays clean and the event is simply ignored until a dedicated renderer ships. Update (ruling 54): the TUI now renders the turn diff. Residual, named: the per-file cap (200 lines) and total cap (400 lines) are fixed constants. Tests: `app/tests/core/turn-diff.test.ts` (11, the aggregator with real temp files for the `after` read and `__resetJournalForTests()` between cases) and `app/tests/core/turn-diff-loop.test.ts` (3, the loop wiring: exactly one `turn.diff` with `files===1` for a one-write turn via a mock provider + captured callbacks, none for a text-only turn, none for a plan-mode staged write). Evidence: `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors; `bun test tests/core/turn-diff.test.ts tests/core/turn-diff-loop.test.ts` → 14 pass; full suite (historical, wave-era) 865 pass / 13 fail (851→865 is the 14 new tests; the 13 fails are the pre-existing root/uid-0 permission artifacts - ruling 39 - not regressions).
47. **Speculative execution coordinator, off by default (Tier-3; generalizes NF-072).** `app/src/core/speculation.ts` is a self-contained, host-injected coordinator that may run ONLY provably side-effect-free tool calls AHEAD of commit, so a batch's reads can overlap the work that decides whether their results are still wanted. It generalizes the NF-072 within-batch parallelism (`batchSegments` + the `Promise.all` batch run in `loop.ts`): NF-072 runs shared calls concurrently once the model has committed; speculation runs the side-effect-free ones eagerly and reconciles them against what was actually committed. **The safety invariant and its mechanism.** Speculation must NEVER run a non-side-effect-free tool and NEVER bypass the kernel guard, and the CLOSED effect union is what guarantees both: a candidate's effect is EXACTLY `{ kind: 'pure' }` or `{ kind: 'local_read'; resources: [{ scheme:'file', path, access:'read' }] }` and nothing else, and `normalizeSpeculationEffect` REJECTS anything else with a typed `SpeculationError` (reason `unspeculable-effect`). A write/edit/shell/bash/agent/code_mode/preview/undo/mcp effect has no representable shape in the union, so such a tool can never become a candidate; `speculationEffectForToolCall` returns `undefined` for every tool except `read`/`grep`/`glob`/`repo_map` (whose `local_read` target is resolved exactly as `guardActionFor` resolves it). **The guard is still consulted.** The coordinator itself never touches the filesystem and never spawns — the ONLY way an effect physically happens is the host-injected `run` callback, which `loop.ts:buildSpeculationHost` wires to the SAME guarded `executeTool` path (`admissionFor -> gate.check -> tool.execute`). A speculatively-run read is therefore a guarded `fs.read`, byte-identical to a non-speculative one; `app/tests/core/speculation.test.ts` proves it by recording that the `fs.read` admission passed through `gate.check` before the read tool ran. **Bounding + reconciliation.** `DEFAULT_MAX_IN_FLIGHT = 2` (admission beyond the bound rejects with reason `max-in-flight`); the fingerprint is `sha256Hex(canonicalJson({ name, args, effect }))` (`core/canon.ts`); states are `queued|running|completed|failed|discarded`; a fingerprint mismatch at reconcile discards the candidate (reason `fingerprint-mismatch`) and the discard CASCADES to every transitive dependent; a host `validate` veto discards and cascades the same way. **Off-by-default decision, and what is NOT wired.** **NOT WIRED (primitives only).** `LoopDeps.speculativeExecution?` is declared but NOTHING in the turn path reads it: the loop never constructs a coordinator, `buildSpeculationHost` and `speculativeCandidatesFor` have no caller outside tests, and no code makes a kept speculative result replace the real `runToolCall`, so enabling the flag today changes nothing. Before wiring, a consumer must define how a kept result short-circuits the real call and emits receipts and `tool.call`/`tool.result` events exactly once. The speculative host is already hardened for that: user pre/post hooks are skipped and an `ask` decision is declined without prompting (an approval request/decision event is still logged); the guard check is never skipped. Coordinator hardening: a discard issued while `run` is in flight is final (not resurrected), a throwing `run`/`validate` records `failed`, a candidate with an unknown or discarded dependency is discarded without running, and duplicate ids are rejected. The loop seam is a single ADDITIVE optional field `LoopDeps.speculativeExecution?: boolean` (default absent/false). The NF-072 batch run is UNCHANGED regardless of the flag, so turn semantics are byte-identical to today. No new type crosses `contracts.ts`: `SpeculationEffect`/`SpeculativeToolCandidate`/`SpeculationError` stay local to `speculation.ts`, and the only loop change is the additive optional `LoopDeps` field plus the exported helpers (`buildSpeculationHost`, `speculativeCandidatesFor`, `RunFaults`/`GuardGate` re-use) — no `GuardAction`/`Tool`/`ToolContext` shape changed. Evidence: `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors; `bun test tests/core/speculation.test.ts` → 22 pass. Residual, named: the loop's on-path integration is NOT done (only the host + candidate builder exist); an aggressive always-on speculator and cross-segment dependency inference are deliberately out of scope (correctness + a clean mechanism over aggressive speculation).
48. **Guardian action classifier, advisory by default (Tier-3).** `app/src/core/guardian.ts` is an OPTIONAL per-action pass: `reviewAction(proposed, instructions, classifier, opts?)` returns a `GuardianJudgment { verdict: 'allow'|'flag'|'deny', reason, score? }` plus `mode`, `blocked`, `surfaced`. **Advisory by default, never silently vetoes:** with `mode` unset or `'advisory'` a `deny`/`flag` is surfaced but `blocked` is always false. **Opt-in enforcing:** only an explicit `{ mode: 'enforce' }` (or `HORIZON_GUARDIAN=enforce` via `guardianModeFromEnv`) makes a `deny` set `blocked: true`; `flag` never blocks, and a classifier that throws degrades to a `flag` (`classifier-error:<code>`), never a veto. **Classifiers:** `deterministicClassifier` (rule based, no provider, for tests and fallback) and `createGuardianClassifier({ model })`, CAPABILITY-DETECTED: model-backed only when a `GuardianModelCapability { complete(prompt) }` is present, else the deterministic classifier; malformed model output raises a typed `GuardianError` (`invalid-model-output`). **Extends review.ts, does not duplicate it:** reuses `REVIEW_FORK_ORIGIN`, the closed read-only `REVIEW_FORK_TOOLS` posture and the typed-error pattern; review.ts is UNCHANGED. Not wired into `loop.ts` (host-invoked opt-in; `guardianModeFromEnv` has no caller yet). The deterministic rules are an advisory fallback with easily evaded patterns, not protection, and the model prompt embeds the target unescaped (only `deny` can block, so this affects advisory signal quality only), so turn semantics are unchanged. No `contracts.ts` change (types local; `GuardAction`/`GuardKind` only consumed). Evidence: `bun run typecheck` 0 errors; `bun test tests/core/guardian.test.ts` 8 pass; full `bun test` 865 pass / 13 fail (same known root-sandbox failures, zero new). Residual: loop wiring and a provider-backed `GuardianModelCapability` adapter are left to the host.
49. **Structural tools admission (Tier-3).** `core/loop.ts` `guardActionFor` maps `ast_search` to `fs.read` (root = `path`, like grep) and `ast_edit` to `fs.write` (target = `path`, like edit); an unmapped registered tool is refused fail-closed, and `tests/core/authority-response.test.ts` requires sample args for every registered tool (both added). The external-backend spawn is a second, inner `shell.exec` admission inside `ast_search` before any `spawnOwned`. Neither tool is in `BASE_TOOL_NAMES`. Matcher correctness rules (review v1): `$$$` matches shortest-first over bracket-balanced `() [] {}` spans; the lexer has three families (C-like default, Rust lifetimes, `#`-comment languages such as Python) chosen by `lang` or file extension; `ast_edit` FAILS (writes nothing) on more than 1000 matches, on an oversize source, and on strings the lexer cannot close on one line, rather than reporting a partial or unscanned rewrite; `ast_search` reports oversize files as skipped. The external ast-grep path applies the same glob / skip-dir filters, is bounded (30s timeout, 8MB output), and DEGRADES to the built-in matcher with a notice on a `shell.exec` deny/decline, nonzero exit, timeout, overflow or unparseable output (a deny still means no spawn). Review v2: every scan has a work budget (`matchBudget.maxSteps`, 10M matcher steps); exhaustion yields `skipped: "budget"`, so `ast_edit` FAILS without writing and `ast_search` prints a `[skipped: N file(s) ... matcher work budget]` notice rather than `No matches.`; `ast_search` also prints `[lexing uncertain in N file(s)]` for files with unterminated strings; `lang` is documented as the lexer-family selector. Review v3: the work budget is shared across a whole `ast_search` call (one counter passed to every file scan), so total stall is bounded regardless of file count; once spent, remaining files are counted as skipped without scanning, and the notice reads `[skipped: N file(s) not scanned or cut short by the matcher work budget (pattern too ambiguous or file too repetitive); results are incomplete]`.
50. **Integration seam fixes (review 2026-11-09-175105).** (a) Plan mode now refuses `apply_patch`, `ast_edit` and `shell_session` with a typed result (`PLAN_MODE_REFUSED`); they are not staged, because staging only models a single-file write/edit projection. Models routed to `apply_patch` therefore cannot stage edits in plan mode; staging multi-file patches is follow-up. (b) The `tool_execution_failed` mistake rail no longer counts expected failures: guard and hook denials, user declines, plan-mode refusals, misses from `edit`/`apply_patch`/`ast_edit`, and empty `tool_search`. They neither count nor reset the tracker; runaway retries are still bounded by loop detection (soft@3, hard@5) and the step budget. (c) The per-session tool-discovery registry is cleared at session end (`clearDiscoveredTools`). (d) **Closed (stage 1, ruling 55).** `shell_session` admitted the command at `start` only, so approving an interpreter (`bash`, `python3`) authorised whatever was typed into it later. Every `write` is now admitted as a `shell.exec` effect whose target is the typed input line(s); see ruling 55. NF-016 item 11 (OS-level confinement) is unchanged. (e) **Not fixed, recorded:** `LoopDeps.speculativeExecution` stays an inert, off-by-default seam (ruling 47) and is not exposed through config or CLI. Tests: `app/tests/core/integration-seams.test.ts` (6 cases; 5 fail with the loop fix reverted). Status: TESTED.
51. **Host-independent tests and a truthful sandbox report (hosted-CI finding, 2026-11-09).** The first hosted CI run that got past install and typecheck failed `rust-kernel-suite` on 4 `sandbox.rs` tests that had asserted the live host's capabilities; the runner (ubuntu-latest, non-root, Landlock active) is a different host class from the dev sandbox (root, no Landlock LSM). Root cause and fix are in the ruling 44 correction: the ladder now takes capabilities and `AppliedMechanisms` as parameters, never reports a dimension `enforced` unless the kernel applies a mechanism for it (none today), and reports detection additively (`available`, `hostCapabilities`). The same sweep, run for real as uid 1000 (`setpriv --reuid=1000 --regid=1000 --clear-groups`, writable HOME/CARGO_HOME, scratch copy), found and fixed four more host dependences: (a) `runKernelNdjson` did not pass a mutated `process.env` to the kernel (3 keyed-anchor interop tests failed for every user; ruling 39 correction); (b) `shell-minimiser.test.ts` used `printf '\u202E'`, whose output depends on the shell's locale (a C locale prints the escape literally), now octal UTF-8 bytes; (c) bun 1.2.x fs errors carry no path (`EACCES: permission denied, open`), so the run-level "receipt issuance failed" message could not name the unwritable store: the fallback's chain appends now go through `appendChain`, which appends the file path when the runtime omits it and keeps the errno code (a genuine product defect, not only a test issue); (d) the 8 chmod-based tests are skipped by a behavioural probe where permission bits do not bind (root), instead of failing. The kernel's selftest comment and check no longer assume a no-Landlock host. Nothing else in `kernel/` or `app/` tests asserts host capabilities: `script`, `git` and `ast-grep` users already probe and skip or stub, and fake executables use `/usr/bin/bash`, present on ubuntu-latest. Evidence: `cargo test --locked` 49 + 50 pass as root and as uid 1000; `bun test` 1330/0 as uid 1000 and 1326 pass / 8 skip / 0 fail as root; `bun run typecheck` 0 errors.
52. **Flaky `guard_denies_writes_inside_the_state_dir_even_when_policy_allows` (hosted CI on main `606f981`, 2026-11-09).** The identical tree passed on the PR run and failed once on the main push run (`kernel closed stdout before answering a request`, 49 pass / 1 fail). Root cause: a test-harness race. The kernel validates `<state>/policy.json` once at startup and exits 2 on a malformed file (`main.rs`, `guard::validate_policy`); that test (and 7 siblings) spawned the kernel and then called `fs::write(policy.json)` with no handshake, and `fs::write` truncates before it writes, so a kernel that reached startup validation in that window read an empty file and exited. Evidence: the mechanism reproduces on demand (a 5 ms truncate-then-write window after spawn makes the kernel exit before replying; with the panic diagnostic added it reads `kernel exited with exit status: 2; stderr: "... policy.json is malformed ..."`); the natural race did NOT reproduce locally (0 failures in 150 filtered runs, 40 full-suite runs at 16 threads and 110 more under 16 busy loops; the window is microseconds), so the failure rate before the fix is unmeasured here and was 1 in 2 hosted runs. Fix (test harness only; kernel, canon.rs and the wire contract unchanged): `Kernel::start_with_env` now sends a readiness `ping` (id 0, outside the `next_id` range) and waits for the reply, which proves startup validation finished before any test writes policy.json (later policy reads happen per request, in sequence); the early-close panic now includes the child's exit status and its captured stderr (stderr goes to a per-process file instead of being inherited). Tests that spawn the binary directly to assert exit 2 are unaffected. After: `cargo test --locked` 49 + 50 pass; the full integration suite passed 60/60 at default threads and 50/50 at 16 threads under 16 busy loops. Hosted CI on main has not yet been seen green; this is TESTED locally, not VERIFIED on hosted CI.
53. **Live serve stdin, real hello kernelMode, honest /files, an exit on every turn (stage 1, 2026-11-09).** (a) `hzcode serve` replied only when the client closed stdin: Bun 1.2.x delivers pipe stdin (process.stdin, readline, `Bun.stdin.stream()`) at EOF, so an interactive client saw nothing. `app/src/core/stdin-worker.ts` now does blocking `readSync` on fd 0 in a Worker and `stdin-live.ts` exposes it as an async iterable that `main.ts` hands to `createServer`; each line is handled and answered as it arrives. (b) `server.hello` reported `kernelMode: "unknown"` until a session opened. It now reports the mode `createRuntime` would use (`resolveKernelBinary()` found: `trusted`, else `untrusted`; no state dir touched), and a session opened later reports the measured mode. The prediction is not a probe: a kernel binary that is present but fails to start still shows `trusted` until the first session. (c) `/files` printed only the model-visible direct tools, so the count did not match the 18 registered. `describeTools` in `tui/interactive.ts` now buckets every registered tool exactly once: available now, deferred (found with `tool_search`), or hidden by model tool routing for the active model; the line reads `tools (N registered): M available now: ...`. (d) `HzRuntimeImpl.send` returned no `exit` on its closed-runtime and runtime-error paths, and the chat bar never showed how a turn ended. Every path now returns a `RunExit` (plan-mode turns included), and the chat bar prints `turn ended: <terminal> (<detail>)` for any exit other than a clean `turn_end`. No `contracts.ts` change. Evidence: `app/tests/core/s1-serve-files-exit.test.ts` (serve over a real OS pipe with stdin held open, which fails if `main.ts` goes back to `process.stdin`; hello mode with `HZ_KERNEL_BIN`; /files reconciliation; exit on plan-mode and closed-runtime turns); `bun run typecheck` 0 errors.
54. **Nested subagent events and the rendered turn diff (stage 1, 2026-11-09).** (a) `runNested` used to swallow every child event (`onEvent: () => undefined`), so a delegated run looked like one silent `agent` card. `app/src/contracts.ts` gains ONE ADDITIVE `RuntimeEvent` variant `{ type: "subagent.event"; agentId; name; depth; event }`, justified in-comment as a harness-side facade event exactly like `turn.diff` and `context.usage` (ephemeral, never written to the log, no seq/hash chain), so the CLOSED 44-name kernel catalogue (`docs/_conventions.md` §6) is UNCHANGED. `core/subagent.ts` forwards a BOUNDED subset of the child's events: only `tool.card`, `notice`, `error` and the child's final `assistant.done` pass (`boundChildEvent`); deltas, usage, diffs and turn boundaries are dropped; text fields are cut to 300 characters, oversized tool arguments are replaced by a truncated preview, and at most 40 events are forwarded per child run (the 41st becomes a single `child events capped` notice). `agentId` is the child session id, `depth` is always 1 (no recursion). `core/index.ts` passes the parent's `emit` as the sink, so `hzcode serve` relays the events as ordinary `session.event` notifications with no server change. A throwing sink never fails the delegated run. `tui/render.ts` renders them under the agent card behind a dim bar (`  │ ⏺ read(...)`, `  │   └ ok: ...`), at most 30 lines per child with a `… more child output hidden` marker. (b) `tui/render.ts` now renders `turn.diff` (ruling 46 had left it ignored): a dim `· changed N file(s)` header, the diff in the existing `diffBlock` style capped at 40 lines, and a dim `… N more lines` marker; `files < 1` prints nothing. HEADLESS decision: stdout is the transcript and is often a pipe, so `tui/headless.ts` prints only the `· changed N file(s)` summary, on STDERR, and never the diff body (full diffs remain available to the server and interactive surfaces). Tests: `tests/core/subagent-events.test.ts` (runtime forwarding, server `session.event` relay, bounds), `tests/tui/render.test.ts` (nested cards, nested cap, turn.diff summary + cap), `tests/tui/headless-diff.test.ts` (stderr summary, clean stdout); each fails when the corresponding change is reverted. Evidence: `cd app && bun run typecheck` → 0 errors; `bun test` → 1272 pass / 8 skip / 0 fail as root.

55. **`shell_session` input is admitted per line (stage 1, closes ruling 50(d)).** Approving a `start` of an interpreter (`bash`, `python3`) used to authorise everything typed into it later, and an allowlisted interpreter made the session silent. Now `start` covers only the start command: every `write` is admitted again through `ctx.guard` as `shell.exec` with the input line as target (blank lines need no admission). Multi-line input is admitted line by line and one refused line refuses the whole write; only COMPLETE lines are admitted: the unterminated tail is carried (every line, complete or not, is capped at 4000 BYTES of UTF-8, not characters: a Linux tty in canonical mode drops input past ~4095 bytes per line, confirmed on a real `script` PTY with bash where 4000 ran intact and 4100 was cut, so longer lines are refused; the approval prompt collapses whitespace but never clips, and the terminal wraps it, so the full executable text is always visible) and admitted once a later write completes it, so a command split across writes (`rm -rf` then ` x`) is admitted as the joined line and a fragment never prompts on its own. Input containing C0 control characters or DEL other than tab/CR/LF (Ctrl-U, Ctrl-W, backspace, Ctrl-C/D, ESC sequences) is refused, because a PTY's line discipline and readline would let the shell run something other than the admitted text; the carried tail is updated only after the stdin write succeeds; the approval prompt shows the whole line so approved text equals executed text (the `always` target stays the exact line, so a typed secret can still become an exact-line grant if the user answers always; known residual). Deny rules and allowlists apply per typed command; an ask prompts `Send input to interactive session` with `<session command> <- <input>` and a decline writes nothing to stdin; the action given to approval is the exact line, so an `always` answer mints the usual exact-target grant (never a prefix, and none for text ending in `*`). Non-interactive stdin readers such as `cat` still work (each input line is prompted unless allowed). `loop.ts` keeps `write`/`close` as session-state (one call can carry several lines, so the tool, not the table, admits them); no `contracts.ts` change. Residual: what the interpreter does with admitted text (a script it runs, a command assembled by escape sequences or control characters) is not inspected; OS confinement stays NF-016. Evidence: `app/tests/tools/interactive-exec.test.ts` (new cases incl. PTY-path control-character and real `bash` start cases, fail with the tool change reverted). **`write` input rules (one place, ruling 55).** Checked in this order, all before the guard, and a refusal writes nothing: (1) `\r\n` and bare `\r` are normalized to `\n` first, so the lines admitted are exactly the lines the shell receives (a piped `bash` treats `\r` as an ordinary word character and would otherwise run `echo A\recho B` as one command the guard saw as two); (2) C0 control characters and DEL other than tab and newline are refused; (3) invisible or direction-changing Unicode is refused (bidi U+202A-202E, U+2066-2069, U+200E/F; zero-width U+200B-200D, U+2060, U+FEFF; U+2028/2029), because it makes the displayed line differ from the executed one; the one-shot `shell` tool refuses the same characters in `command`; (4) a single write is capped at 64 lines and 64 KiB, which bounds the approval prompts one write can raise; (5) every line, complete or not, is capped at 4000 UTF-8 bytes (tty canonical limit). `start` refuses the same characters. The TUI approval shows a `shell.exec` detail verbatim (whitespace runs kept, so `cat 'a   b'` reads as it runs; other kinds still collapse whitespace) and renders invisible Unicode AND control characters/ANSI escapes (C0 except tab/newline, DEL, C1) as `<U+XXXX>` instead of stripping them, so nothing in the executed command text is hidden from the human. Residual: the loop admits a one-shot `shell` command (and approval is shown) before the tool refuses invisible Unicode, so such a command can raise a prompt and then be refused. Residual: what an approved interpreter does with admitted text is not inspected (NF-016). Status: TESTED.

56. **Specialised agent presets, bounded by the parent's tools (stage 3, 2026-11-09; CURRENT_RUN 5.0.1, `docs/30` 5.11; contract `AgentProfileV1`, `docs/15` 2.6/3.6, `docs/05` 4.9).** (a) The `agent` tool takes an optional `profile`. Built-ins are DATA in `app/src/core/agent-profiles.ts`: `explore`, `plan`, `review` (read-only: read, grep, glob, repo_map, ast_search, lsp) and `code` (those plus write, edit, shell, todo, apply_patch, ast_edit, undo). Each is a tool allow-list, a prompt block appended to the child prompt, an optional model override and a step cap; the caller's `max_steps` can only lower the cap. No `profile` keeps the generic seven-tool child unchanged. (b) A preset is bounded by the PARENT's tools (D17, profile half, as amended by ruling 61): its allow-list must be a subset of the ceiling, defined as every registered tool except `agent`. "Narrow" is relative to the parent, not to the generic seven-tool child: `code` holds `apply_patch`, `ast_edit`, `undo`, `lsp`, `repo_map` and `ast_search`, which the generic child does not. A user preset naming anything outside it is refused with `profile_ceiling_widens` (`docs/15` section 8) and the delegated call returns that reason; the child never starts. The child runs through the same guard, approvals and receipts as before, with no parent leases (R-tools-32); a preset grants nothing. (c) User presets are `agents/*.md` files with front-matter (`name`, `description`, `tools`, `model`, `max_steps`; body = prompt) read ONLY from the user config dir and the workspace `.hz/`. A file with bad front-matter, a name that collides with a built-in or an earlier preset, or a tool list outside the ceiling is refused and not listed (no silent override). Workspace files are repository content, so a workspace preset's prompt is a prompt-injection surface; it is acceptable only because a preset cannot exceed the parent's tools and the guard still decides every effect. (d) The loop appends the preset catalog (built-in and user, with source tags) to the `agent` tool description each request, so the model knows they exist. (e) `contracts.ts`, ADDITIVE and justified: `DelegateRunner` gains an optional third argument `profile`, and the `subagent.event` variant gains an optional `profile` name; the TUI prints `profile: <name>` once under the agent card before the child's first line. (f) The base prompt gains one line in Tool use (when to delegate: explore for broad search, review before finishing a larger change); the golden fixture changed with it and the lean-prompt estimate is 696 of 700. Tests: `app/tests/core/agent-profiles.test.ts` (built-in shape, narrowing, parser refusals, user/workspace loading, catalog in the parent request, child tool list and a refused `write`, widening and unknown profile refusals, card name, base prompt line). Not done: result schemas, `requiresInputs`/`refusesWhen`, pinning `{id, revision, digest}`, kernel-side profile validation, plugin-provided profiles, and per-profile cheap-model routing beyond the optional `model` string.

57. **Headless print mode, `-p` / `--print` (stage 3, 2026-11-09; CURRENT_RUN headless row; `docs/14` §3).** (a) `hzcode -p [text]` sends ONE turn and exits; the prompt is the `-p` text (plus any positional words) or, when absent, all of stdin; an empty prompt is a usage error (2). `--output-format text` (default) prints the final assistant text; `json` prints one object `{type:"result", result, sessionId, exit:{terminal, detail?, code}, usage, toolCalls, changedFiles}`; `stream-json` prints each RuntimeEvent as one NDJSON line, then the same object. `--output-format` without `-p`, or an unknown format, is exit 2. (b) The exit code is `exitCodeFor` (S16: not restated), 130/143 on SIGINT/SIGTERM. (c) `usage` is the harness estimate from the last `context.usage` event (`{model, contextWindow, estimatedInputTokens}`), null if none was emitted, NOT provider-billed tokens. `changedFiles` is parsed from `turn.diff` bodies, so a diff clipped by its line cap can omit files. (d) Approvals stay fail closed: `-p` forces the non-interactive approval UI even on a TTY. (e) `contracts.ts` is unchanged (the result type lives in `app/src/tui/print.ts`); the line-per-message mode is untouched. Tests: `app/tests/tui/print.test.ts`.

58. **Named permission modes (stage 3, 2026-11-09; CURRENT_RUN permission-modes row; `docs/11` mode note).** (a) `app/src/core/permission-mode.ts` defines `default | plan | accept-edits | read-only`. It is a policy layer ABOVE the kernel guard, applied inside `GuardGate` (`loop.ts`): it can refuse (read-only) or answer an ASK (accept-edits) and can never produce an allow the guard did not give; a kernel `deny` (including `kernel-state-protected`) returns before the mode could answer and is final. (b) `default` is today's behaviour. `plan` is the existing plan mode (`/mode plan` and `/plan on` are one flag). `read-only` denies every `fs.write`, `shell.exec` and (ruling 61) `mcp.invoke` before the cache and the kernel, even where the shipped policy would allow an in-workspace write (reason `read-only permission mode refuses <kind>`); reads and the other kinds are unchanged. `accept-edits` answers yes, without calling the approval handler, to an `fs.write` ask whose target resolves INSIDE the workspace (symlink-aware, `pathInsideWorkspace`) and outside the sensitive set of ruling 61 (`.git`, `.hz`, CI/hook directories, manifests, shell rc and env files); shell, net.fetch, spawn, MCP, every outside-workspace write and every read ask stay exactly as default (fail-closed deny under `-p`). Named residual: the shipped DEFAULT_POLICY already allows in-workspace writes, so accept-edits changes behaviour only where a user `policy.json` makes them ask (or for the `.hz`/`.git` carve-out, which it refuses to answer); that is why it is honest to describe it as a guard against a stricter policy becoming a prompt storm, not as a widening. (c) Sources: `--permission-mode <m>` (unknown name: exit 2), else the USER config file key `permissionMode` (a bad value is ignored and named in a notice), else `default`. No workspace file is read for the mode, so a repository can never raise it; there is no workspace narrowing key either (narrowing a run is `--permission-mode read-only`). `/mode [name]` is session-only and nothing ever writes a mode to disk. A subagent receives the parent's mode (`runNested`), so a child is never looser. (d) Display: a non-default mode prints the prompt badge (`PLAN`, `ACCEPT-EDITS`, `READ-ONLY`, bold yellow) before the `❯`, one notice at chat start, and one stderr line under `-p`/pipes. `-p --permission-mode accept-edits` therefore honours the mode through the same gate. (e) **No bypass/yolo mode, by decision.** A mode that answers every ask would also answer net.fetch (exfiltration), spawn, MCP and shell asks, leaving only the kernel deny list, which covers a fixed set of paths and not intent; the kernel-state protection alone cannot make that safe. `yolo`, `bypass`, `bypassPermissions`, `dangerously-skip-permissions` are refused in the flag, `/mode` and config with that explanation. (f) Additive contract: `RuntimeOptions.permissionMode`. Runtime extras `permissionMode()` / `setPermissionMode()` are feature-detected by the TUI like the plan hooks. Not covered: `hzcode serve` does not take a mode yet; the user-typed `/apply` and `/rewind` paths still use the ordinary approval handler and are not narrowed by read-only. Tests: `app/tests/core/permission-mode.test.ts`, `app/tests/tui/permission-mode.test.ts`.

59. **Session fork (stage 3, 2026-11-09; docs/12 S-45).** (a) `/fork [n]` and the server method `session.fork` create a NEW session holding a copy of the parent's transcript, whole or up to a message index (`/fork n` uses checkpoint n's `messageIndex`, `session.fork {sessionId, at?}` takes the index). The cut is boundary-safe (`safeTruncationBoundary`): a fork never starts between an assistant tool_call and its results, so the copy can be shorter than asked and the real count is reported. Message ids are fresh; order and timestamps are kept; the parent's rows are only read, so the original is untouched. (b) Lineage lives on the child's `sessions` row (nullable columns `forked_from`, `fork_index`, `fork_seq`, added in place on open, no breakage for older databases) and is returned as `SessionInfo.forkedFrom {sessionId, messageIndex, parentSeq}`; `session.list` rows and `/sessions` show it. (c) The kernel event log stays append-only: a fork is a new stream `session:<childId>` whose FIRST event is `session.fork` (`EventTypes.sessionFork`, payload `SessionForkPayload {parentSessionId, parentStream, parentSeq, messageIndex}`; `parentSeq` is the parent stream head read at fork time). The parent stream is only read. (d) contracts.ts additions, all additive: `EventTypes.sessionFork`, `SessionForkPayload`, `"session.fork"` in `ServerMethod` and `SERVER_METHODS`, and `messageIndex` on the checkpoint view (so `/fork n` can cut there). (e) Runtime seam: `forkSession(at?)` creates the child without switching; `switchSession(id)` moves the runtime onto an existing session (running the same transcript repair as open); the server resumes the child through the ordinary by-id path, so resume works on forks (`hzcode --resume <id>` too). (f) Not done: posture narrowing for forks (docs/12 S-46; a fork inherits the process's current mode and config, as `/new` does), fork-of-fork depth limits, and a `hzcode session fork` CLI verb. Tests: `tests/core/session-fork.test.ts`, `tests/tui/fork.test.ts` (fail with the implementation stashed).

60. **Guardian surfacing, advisory (stage 3, 2026-11-09; supersedes the "not wired" part of ruling 48; CURRENT_RUN guardian row).** (a) `GuardGate.approve` (`loop.ts`) runs `deterministicClassifier` on an ASKED action and attaches a one-line advisory to the approval request: `ApprovalRequest.guardian` (additive optional field in `contracts.ts`), rendered by the TUI under the prompt as `· guardian: flag - privileged or risky shell command` (hyphen, no em dash; invisible Unicode escaped; shown only for flag/deny). (b) ADVISORY BY DEFAULT: it never blocks and never turns anything into an allow; it runs only after the kernel said "ask", so a kernel deny is untouched. `HORIZON_GUARDIAN=enforce` makes a guardian `deny` refuse the ask without prompting (a flag still asks); a classifier failure fails open to flag. (c) The model-backed prompt (`guardianPrompt`) now JSON-encodes the task instructions and the proposed action inside `<data>` tags and escapes `<` and `>`, so untrusted text cannot forge a delimiter (the review found them embedded raw). The model classifier is still only used when a host supplies a `GuardianModelCapability`; the gate uses the deterministic one. The deterministic patterns are easily evaded and are not protection. Evidence: `bun test tests/core/guardian-surfacing.test.ts tests/tui/approval.test.ts`.

61. **Review fixes for rulings 56-60 (stage 3, 2026-11-09).** (a) *Preset ceiling, claim corrected.* The ceiling stays "every registered tool except `agent`" (the parent's tools). The earlier "only narrows" wording overstated it, because the generic child holds only the base seven and `code`/user presets can hold more. Code, tool description, docs 09/11/15 and README now say a preset picks a subset of the parent's tools; the guard still decides every effect and a preset grants no lease. Workspace presets (`.hz/agents/*.md`) stay allowed and labelled `[workspace]` in the catalog; a workspace preset cannot set `model` (the key is ignored when the source is `workspace`; only built-in and user-dir presets may, and a user preset's `model` is trusted user config and not validated against a catalog, an unknown model failing at the provider and not as an authority change) and their text is a documented injection surface (56c). Not changed: user-dir-only presets. (b) *accept-edits scope.* The sensitive set is now directories at any depth (`.git`, `.hz`, `.github`, `.gitlab`, `.husky`, `.circleci`, `.devcontainer`, `.vscode`) and file names at any depth, case-insensitive (`package.json`, `.envrc`, `.env` and `.env.*`, `.npmrc`, shell rc/profile files, `Makefile`, `Dockerfile`, compose files, CI configs, `setup.py`, `build.rs` and similar): writing one is deferred code execution, so a human answers it. The guardian review now runs BEFORE the accept-edits auto-answer, so `HORIZON_GUARDIAN=enforce` refuses a guardian deny in that mode as well. The list is a denylist and not complete; it is a guard against surprise, not a sandbox. (c) *read-only coverage.* `mcp.invoke` is refused with `fs.write` and `shell.exec`, since an MCP tool can write. `net.fetch` (a read), LSP/MCP server start and `agent`/`code_mode` spawns are left to the guard: a child inherits the mode, and each `code_mode` bridge call is admitted on its own merits, so none can write or run a command. (d) *Plan mode and children.* No change needed: while plan mode is on the loop refuses the `agent` tool outright (`PLAN_MODE_REFUSED`), so no child exists to be looser; a test pins it and the `runNested` comment says so. (e) *Fork mid-turn.* `forkSession` now refuses while a turn is running or a `send` is queued (the TUI already did); a failed `session.fork` event append no longer fails the fork (the store row already carries the lineage; the user gets a notice), and resume never reads that event. (f) *Em dashes.* Removed from the two `/fork` notices and the eight new file headers. (g) *Revert evidence.* Each new case was run against the stashed source and fails, except the plan-mode case, which pins existing behaviour; the `delimit` and `session.fork` stream-event tests also fail when those pieces are reverted. Tests: `app/tests/core/permission-mode.test.ts` (review fixes block), `app/tests/core/session-fork.test.ts`.

62. **accept-edits path judgement hardened (stage 3 v3 review, 2026-11-09).** `modeAutoApproves` now judges a write target by BOTH its lexical path and its symlink-resolved path (`realpathish`), and matches sensitive directory names case-insensitively. Before this, an in-workspace link such as `gitlink -> .git` or `notes.md -> package.json` hid a sensitive target from the check, and `.GIT/hooks` was not recognised on a case-insensitive filesystem; both were auto-answered. Tests: `permission-mode.test.ts` ("v3: ..."). A kernel deny is unaffected (it returns before the mode is consulted).

63. **Cross-process advisory locking (runtime-depth FEAT-002).** New `app/src/core/file-lock.ts`: an `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` lockfile holding `pid\ntoken`, with stale detection by pid liveness (`process.kill(pid, 0)`; EPERM counts as alive), a grace window for empty lock files left by a crash between create and write, a bounded wait (`LockTimeoutError`, `HZ_LOCK_TIMEOUT`, names the live holder) and token-checked release. Wired around: the session DB schema + ALTER migration (`session.ts`, `<db>.migrate.lock`), checkpoint appends (`checkpoints.ts`), change-journal appends and compaction (`change-journal.ts`; compaction re-reads the file under the lock so another process's records are never rewritten away), every TS-kernel append (`LocalKernel.withLock` takes `<state>/locks/<key>.lock` after its in-process queue, so seq/prevHash cannot interleave across processes), and a per-target-file lock (`<state root>/locks/<sha256(path)>.lock`) around verify -> write -> journal in `write`, `edit`, `apply_patch` and `ast_edit`. The edit lock is taken AFTER approval, never across a human decision. A change made by another process before the lock is acquired is caught by the existing sha256 preimage / snapshot check and reported `HZ_PRECONDITION_FAILED: ... preimage changed`; it is not clobbered. Fail-open rule: when the lock file cannot be created for a reason other than "exists" (read-only directory), the work runs unlocked so the caller's own honest error surfaces. Named residuals: advisory only (a non-cooperating editor is caught by the sha256 check, not the lock); pid liveness is host-local (not for NFS workspaces across hosts); pid reuse can keep a dead holder looking alive until timeout; `apply_patch` move locks source then destination, so two processes moving a->b and b->a time out rather than deadlock; the Rust kernel's own appends are untouched (canon.rs and the kernel are unchanged). Tests: `tests/core/file-lock.test.ts` (unit + real `Bun.spawn` child processes via `tests/core/fixtures/lock-worker.ts`).

---

64. **Hashline anchors (runtime-depth FEAT-003).** New pure module `app/src/tools/hashline.ts`, after grok-build `grok_build_hashline/scheme.rs` (`research/compare-codex-grok.md` section 2.2). An anchor is `LINE:LOCAL:CONTEXT`: LOCAL is three letters hashing the line with all whitespace removed (an indent change does not stale it), CONTEXT is three letters hashing the previous and next lines' LOCAL hashes (a repeated `}` is not a free pass). This is a deliberate variant of grok's ChunkFingerprint: a chunk fingerprint changes whenever an insertion moves a chunk boundary, which would defeat shift recovery. `read` gains `anchors?: boolean` (default false; the default rendering is the same code path and byte-identical, so the prompt cache is unchanged apart from the one-time schema text). `edit` gains `start_anchor` and optional `end_anchor` in place of `old` (mutually exclusive with `old` and `all`; `required` is now `path`, `new`, and the string mode still reports `old is required`). Each anchor resolves Valid, Stale or OutOfRange; a Stale or OutOfRange anchor recovers only when exactly one line within +-15 lines validates (LOCAL and CONTEXT), a second candidate is a refusal naming every candidate line, a truncated anchor (no CONTEXT) is Stale and a malformed one does not parse. The whole line range becomes `new` (an empty `new` deletes it); the replacement takes the range's native line ending, so CRLF files stay CRLF. Same discipline as string mode: guard, then approve (when `ask`), then the per-file lock (ruling 63), the preimage sha256 re-check, atomic write, read-back verify and a change-journal record; anchors are resolved against the exact bytes whose sha256 is re-checked. Anchored mode needs no prior-read ledger entry because the anchors are themselves the proof of freshness. Plan mode refuses to stage an anchored edit (only the exact string rung is projectable). Named residuals: LOCAL and CONTEXT are 15 bits each, so a collision can make a stale anchor look valid or a shift look ambiguous (the second is a safe refusal; the first needs a changed line whose neighbours hash identically); anchors are line-only and do not cover mid-line edits. Tests: `tests/tools/hashline.test.ts`.
65. **Speculative execution: decision to leave UNWIRED, flag pinned inert (runtime-depth FEAT-004; supersedes the "consumer must define" note of ruling 47).** `LoopDeps.speculativeExecution` stays off by default and still changes nothing. A kept speculative result cannot soundly REPLACE the real call today, for four reasons found in `loop.ts`. (1) *No commit window.* `streamTurn` collects `turn.toolCalls` and the batch runs only after the provider stream is done, so the model has already committed every call; the only pre-commit window is mid-stream, where events cannot be rolled back. (2) *Ordering.* `batchSegments` keeps reads after an earlier write in a later segment; a read run ahead of that write would return pre-write content, and sound dependency inference across segments is exactly what ruling 47 left out of scope. (3) *Hook bypass.* The speculative host deliberately skips user `tool.pre`/`tool.post` hooks and declines `ask` without prompting; a kept result substituted for the real call would skip a user's pre hook (which may deny) and the approval prompt, a policy bypass, while re-running them defeats the point. (4) *Exactly-once.* `runToolCall` owns `tool.card`, the `tool.call` and `tool.result` log events, the receipt, the lazy-instruction discovery, the transcript row and the mistake/loop rails; splitting it so a precomputed result slots in changes the shared path for every tool. Reads in the same batch already overlap through NF-072, so the remaining gain is small against that risk. Test: `app/tests/core/speculation-unwired.test.ts` runs the same two-read script with the flag absent and on and requires identical guard checks (each `fs.read` exactly once) and `tool.card` counts; a naive wiring that runs the read speculatively and again for real fails it. Wiring is revisited only with a design that keeps `tool.pre` hooks and approval in front of any kept result. Status: DECISION RECORDED, TESTED.
66. **Runtime residuals: shell pre-admission refusal, guardian fail-closed, wider accept-edits list, lazy dedup refresh (runtime-depth FEAT-005).** (a) *Shell (ruling 55).* `executeTool` in `loop.ts` now checks the one-shot `shell` command for invisible Unicode BEFORE `admissionFor`/`gate.check`/`gate.approve`, returning the tool's own refusal text as an expected failure. Before, the guard ask and the approval prompt were raised for a command `shell.execute` then refused. The tool keeps its own check (defense in depth, direct callers). (b) *Guardian (rulings 48, 60).* `reviewAction` in ENFORCE mode now treats a throwing classifier as a `deny` (reason `classifier-error:<code> (guardian enforce mode fails closed)`, `blocked: true`); advisory mode is unchanged and fails OPEN (a `flag`). `GuardGate.approveNow` also refuses the ask when `reviewAction` itself throws and `HORIZON_GUARDIAN=enforce`; advisory still proceeds to the human. An enforcing guardian that could not judge must not wave an action on. This supersedes the earlier "a throwing classifier never blocks, even when enforcing" line of ruling 48. (c) *accept-edits list (ruling 62).* Matching is unchanged: directory names at any depth and file BASENAMES, both lowercased, judged on the lexical and the symlink-resolved path. Added, each because writing it is deferred code execution or swaps the executed binary: `.cargo` as a DIRECTORY (covers `.cargo/config.toml` and legacy `.cargo/config`: `[build] rustc-wrapper`, `[target] runner`, `[alias]` run on the next cargo command; a directory entry also catches any depth and either spelling); `rust-toolchain.toml` and the legacy `rust-toolchain` (rustup toolchain `path` and component selection choose the compiler every cargo call runs); `.tool-versions` (asdf/mise select which executables run); `pyproject.toml` (build backend runs on install, plus script and task tables); `tox.ini` (`commands` run shell lines); `noxfile.py` (Python executed by nox); `rakefile` and `rakefile.rb` (Ruby executed by rake); `gemfile` (Ruby evaluated by bundler). NOT added, on purpose: `go.mod`/`go.sum`/`go.work` (declarative; `replace` and `toolchain` fetch or point at sources but Go runs no code from them, `//go:generate` lives in `.go` files that are ordinary source), `Cargo.toml` (routine edits; its code-running half is `build.rs`, already listed), `Gemfile.lock`. Residual: `mise.toml`, `setup.cfg` and `Pipfile` are not listed. (d) *Lazy instructions (stage 2 residual).* `LazyInstructions` keeps the dedup state derived from the eager pass apart from what it injected itself, and `lazyInstructionsFor` calls the new `refreshEager` on every run for an existing session; eager files are re-read each run, so a mid-session edit no longer leaves the old text in the dedup set (a nested file that only looked like a duplicate was hidden forever; one that now duplicates the new text was injected twice). Visited directories are forgotten on refresh so a previously dropped file is reconsidered; already injected fragments stay protected. Tests: `app/tests/core/runtime-residuals.test.ts` (21 cases, all fail with the `src` changes reverted) and `app/tests/core/guardian.test.ts`. No `contracts.ts` change. Status: TESTED.
67. **Tool-argument JSON repair and schema coercion (forgecode take T-1; NF-069 / docs/10 R15).** New zero-dependency `app/src/core/json-repair.ts`; `loop.ts` `parseArguments(raw, toolName?)` now delegates to it. (a) *Repairs, syntax only, each counted:* trailing commas, single / smart / backtick quotes (a non-ASCII or single quote only closes a string when a structural character follows, so `'it's'` stays content), unquoted keys, a wrapping code fence, raw control characters inside strings, invalid escapes kept literally (`\d` stays `\d`), `True`/`None`/`undefined`, and a closed truncated tail. (b) *Fail closed:* nothing is guessed. A bare word, a missing comma or colon, a value-less key and trailing content are errors; a member whose value was cut off is DROPPED, never completed with `null` (forge completes it). A truncated tail is only closed for the read-only allowlist `TRUNCATION_TOLERANT_TOOLS` (read, grep, glob, repo_map, tool_search, ast_search); for `shell`, `write`, `edit`, `apply_patch`, MCP tools and an unnamed call the blob is refused with a `cut off` error and nothing runs, so a half-sent `command` or `content` is never completed. (c) *Coercion* runs against the tool's own JSON schema and is limited to unambiguous scalars (`"42"` to 42 for number/integer, `"true"`/`"false"` to boolean, a JSON-encoded array/object string to array/object); a string-typed field is never touched. (d) *Audit:* the result carries `repaired: true` plus the repair list; the running and finished `tool.card` events carry `repaired: true` (additive field), the durable `tool.call` event carries `repaired` and `repairs`, and the receipt `argsDigest` is the digest of the REPAIRED args that were admitted (the admitted args are the single source for the guard check, the tool and the receipt). Tests: `tests/core/json-repair.test.ts` (forge fixture corpus ported, with the forge-guesses cases asserted as refusals), `tests/core/json-repair-loop.test.ts` (loop wiring, receipt digest, truncated shell/write refused).
68. **Tool-schema snapshot (forgecode take T-2).** `app/tests/tools/tool-schema-snapshot.test.ts` serialises every builtin tool (name, description, JSON schema, exposure, batch class) with recursively sorted keys and compares it to the committed `app/tests/fixtures/tool-schemas.json`, so a schema or description change is a visible diff (it also changes the prompt-cache prefix). Update path, never automatic: `cd app && HZ_UPDATE_TOOL_SCHEMAS=1 bun test tests/tools/tool-schema-snapshot.test.ts`, then review the fixture diff in the same commit. The test also caps descriptions at 1024 characters (forge's limit), requires unique snake_case names, object schemas with typed properties, and `required` keys that exist. This is the cheap first half of the designed cache-guard test (docs/10).
69. **Pending-todos re-check (forgecode take T-3).** When a model turn ends (no tool calls) while the session todo list (`tools/todo.ts` `openTodos`) has items not done, `runTurn` appends ONE `[pending-todos-reminder]` user message (same marker pattern as `[repeat-tool-reminder]`; at most 5 items named, each text cut at 80 characters, the rest counted) and runs one more step. `todoReminderSent` makes it once per run, never a loop; the next iteration re-checks abort and every budget, so the extra step is not free; with no todos or all done there is no effect. The completion-evidence gate is unchanged and still the terminal: if the model stops again with items open, the end-of-run notice and the `unknown` exit rules apply. The reminder reaches the model as a transcript message and is logged as the `run.pending_todos_reminder` loop event (a harness-side event name, like `run.unverified_completion`). Note: the `todo` tool has no cancel action, so the reminder offers finishing or marking done only. Tests: `tests/core/pending-todos.test.ts`.
70. **Attempts remaining on failed tool results (forgecode take T-4).** A failed tool result that COUNTS as a mistake now ends with `[attempts remaining before this run stops: N]`, derived from `MistakeTracker.remaining` (limit 3, minus the streak, minus this failure) and, for a call whose arguments did not parse, the tighter of that and the format cap (`MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FORMAT_ERRORS`). Reading is 2, 1, 0 across three consecutive failing steps; a success resets it. Not shown for failures that are the system working as designed (guard/hook denials, user declines, plan-mode stages, edit-ladder misses: `expected`/`staged`), and not shown when the limit is disabled. Model-facing text only: the tool's output, the receipt digests and the events are unchanged; no authority is involved. Within one concurrent batch every failed call reports the same pre-batch count (a batch is one mistake). Tests: `tests/core/attempts-remaining.test.ts`.
71. **`hzcode doctor` (forgecode take T-10; Grok `/doctor`).** New `app/src/core/doctor.ts` and a `doctor` subcommand in `main.ts` (dispatched before the chat arg parse, like `serve`; listed in `--help` and the README Quickstart). Checks, each OK / WARN / FAIL with a one-line `fix:` hint on every non-OK line: Bun version (`MIN_BUN` 1.1.0, suite runs on 1.2.x), kernel binary (`resolveKernelBinary`, so `HZ_KERNEL_BIN` stays authoritative) and its `--selftest`, kernel mode (trusted only when the Rust kernel is found and its selftest passes; otherwise the TypeScript fallback is reported as UNTRUSTED, a WARN), config file parse (a broken file is a FAIL because at runtime it is silently ignored), an inline `apiKey` in a group/world-readable config (WARN), provider and key presence (the key value is never printed in either output form; asserted by tests), model resolution against the catalog (unknown is a WARN, since custom endpoints are legitimate), workspace writability via `access(2)`, the kernel state dir and its files being private (WARN with `chmod -R go-rwx`), git, and terminal facts (TTY, colour level via the existing `detectColorLevel`, UTF-8 locale, `NO_COLOR`, `TERM=dumb`). Exit 1 on any FAIL (WARN exits 0), 2 on a usage error; `--json` for the same report as JSON. Diagnose-only: no auto-fix, no network, and the single filesystem touch is the kernel selftest's own scratch dir under the OS temp dir, removed afterwards (a test pins that nothing under the workspace or home changes). A check that throws is reported as a FAIL line, never a crash. Tests: `tests/core/doctor.test.ts` (injected environment per check, plus the real CLI end to end).
72. **Startup-latency gate (forgecode take T-11; forge's rprompt benchmark gate).** `ops/gates.sh` gains an eighth gate, `startup-latency`, last in the fail-fast order: the median of 5 timed runs (after one untimed warm-up) of `hzcode --version` must be at most 500 ms and of one headless mock turn at most 1000 ms (`HZ_LATENCY_VERSION_MS` / `HZ_LATENCY_TURN_MS` override). Measured here (8-core x86_64 container, Bun 1.2.14, root): 96 ms and 198 ms medians, so the ceilings are about 5x, chosen wider than a quiet machine needs because hosted runners are slower and a flaky gate gets ignored; the measurements, the margin argument and the re-measure instruction are in `ops/gate-catalogue.md`. Every timed run must exit 0 and print its expected text (a crash is not "fast"), and a host without a millisecond clock fails the gate instead of passing on whole seconds. About 2 s of gate time. `ops/gates.sh` now defines its functions and runs nothing when sourced, which is how `tests/core/gate-startup-latency.test.ts` drives the gate in isolation (a generous ceiling must pass; a 1 ms ceiling must fail and name the median and the limit). `ci.yml` and `ops/ci/github-actions.yml` are unchanged: CI already calls `bash ops/gates.sh`.
73. **Base prompt names the todo tool and the instruction files' checks (forgecode takes follow-up).** Two `loop` lines in `app/src/core/prompt/sections.ts` change, token-neutral within the 700 cap (estimate 699): multi-step work is tracked with the todo tool and items are marked done (so the ruling 69 reminder fires only on real leftovers), and verification runs the checks the project instruction files (AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md) name before falling back to tests, typecheck or build. The phrase `unverified completion` is kept (coverage test). Golden `tests/core/fixtures/system-prompt.golden.txt` regenerated; the docs/10 rendered prefix now matches `staticPrompt()` byte for byte, including the Agent tool line it had been missing. Also: the JSON repair parser writes keys as own properties (a `"__proto__"` key no longer swaps the parsed object's prototype), matching `JSON.parse`.
